We derived the SOFA method using the first operator splitting simply because it results in less computations for the position component. However, this may be negligible in practice since both splitting approaches require the same number of gradient evaluations per step.
In any case, the choice of method for the shifted ODE is likely to be problem dependent.

The following numerical scheme can be viewed as a variant of the Strang splitting.
Although we expect it only have a first order
\mathbb{L}_{2}
convergence rate, it has the additional advantage that it can be Metropolis-adjusted (in a similar fashion to standard HMC).
Moreover, it was shown in
[
47
]
to achieve a second order
2
-Wasserstein convergence rate.

Definition 4.4

(
(Unadjusted) OBABO scheme

[
4
,
47
,
54
]
)
.

Similar to Strang splitting,
we construct a numerical solution
\big\{\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
for (
1.1
) by setting
\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0}\big):=(x_{0}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{0})

and for
n\geq 0
, defining
\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}\big)
as

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(0)}

\displaystyle:=e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n}+\frac{1}{2}h_{n}}e^{-\gamma(t_{n}+\frac{1}{2}h_{n}-t)}dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 14.93768pt(O)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(1)}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(0)}-\frac{1}{2}u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)\hskip 0.7113pth_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 109.5431pt(B)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(1)}h_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 151.51091pt(A)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(2)}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(1)}-\frac{1}{2}u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}\big)\hskip 0.7113pth_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 99.58464pt(B)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}

\displaystyle:=e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(2)}+\sigma\int_{t_{n}+\frac{1}{2}h_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}e^{-\gamma(t_{n+1}-t)}dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 26.31879pt(O)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\frac{1}{4}\big(\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(0)}+\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(1)}\big)h_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Just as before, the OBABO scheme requires one additional evaluation of
\nabla f
per step.
Its name is derived from the order in which the damping/sampling (O), acceleration (A) and free transport parts (B) of the dynamics are applied. For example, there are also BAOAB and ABOBA methods
[
33
]
(which we view as being similar to Strang splitting).
We note the scheme is straightforward to implement as the stochastic integrals used in the (O) operations are independent (since Brownian motion has independent increments).

We refer the reader to
[
47
,
54
]
for details on the Metropolis-adjusted OBABO scheme.
In particular, the adjusted OBABO scheme was shown to empirically outperform the standard HMC method in
[
54
]
(though it was only the third best performing approach).
Based on their stochastic Taylor expansions, the Strang splitting should outperform the unadjusted OBABO scheme for
\mathbb{L}_{2}
convergence and this is supported by our experiment.
We also note that
\big\{\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+\frac{1}{2}}\big\}
resembles a chain produced by an ABOBA splitting, which has second order
\mathbb{L}_{2}
convergence
[
33
]
.
Since the OBABO scheme and Strang splitting have the same
2
-Wasserstein convergence rates, it is not clear how they would compare in practice.

It was shown in
[
8
]
that the following method achieves an optimal
\mathbb{L}_{2}
convergence rate of
\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pth^{1.5}\big)
under minimal regularity assumptions (i.e.
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz continuous).
Unlike the previously discussed methods, here the gradient
\nabla f
is evaluated at a uniformly sampled point within each interval
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
(referred to as the “randomized midpoint”).

Definition 4.5

(
Randomized midpoint method

[
52
,
31
]
)
.

Let
\{\alpha_{n}\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
be a sequence of independent random variables with
\alpha_{n}\sim U[0,1]
. We construct a numerical solution
\big\{\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)\big\}_{n\geq 0}
by setting
\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0}\big):=(x_{0}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{0})

and for
n\geq 0
, defining
\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}\big)
as

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma\alpha_{n}h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-u\bigg(\frac{e^{-\gamma\alpha_{n}h_{n}}+\gamma\hskip 0.7113pt\alpha_{n}h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}}\bigg)\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 31.44029pt+\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n}+\alpha_{n}h_{n}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dW_{s}\hskip 0.7113ptdt,

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-uh_{n}\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma(1-\alpha_{n})h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dW_{s}\hskip 0.7113ptdt,

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}

\displaystyle:=e^{-\gamma h_{n}}\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-u\big(e^{-\gamma(1-\alpha_{n})h_{n}}h_{n}\big)\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{(1)}\big)+\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}e^{-\gamma(t_{n+1}-t)}dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Unlike the previously discussed approaches, the randomized midpoint method does not exploit the smoothness of
f
and requires two additional evaluations of
\nabla f
per step.
However, if the gradient
\nabla f
is only Lipschitz continuous, the randomized midpoint method should outperform the other unadjusted Langevin MCMC methods discussed in this paper.
In our numerical experiment, the randomized midpoint method demonstrates
O(h^{1.5})
convergence, but is outperformed by the SORT, SOFA and Strang splitting methods since
the target log-density comes from a logistic regression and is thus infinitely differentiable.
Generating the stochastic integrals is also straightforward and is detailed in Appendix
E
.

The following numerical method for ULD (and the error analysis used to analyse it)
inspired much of our research. Since it does not yet appear to have a widely used name, we shall refer to this approach as the “left-point method”. The left-point method only requires one evaluation of
\nabla f
per step and achieves an
\mathbb{L}_{2}
convergence rate of
\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pth\big)
.
It is obtained by solving (
1.1
) exactly with
\nabla f(x_{t})
assumed to be constant on
[t_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,t_{n+1}]
.
Thus, it differs from the standard Euler-Maruyama method as only the
\nabla f
term is fixed.

Definition 4.6

(
Left-point method

[
12
,
16
,
11
]
)
.

We define a numerical solution

\big\{\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)\big\}

for the SDE (
1.1
) by setting
\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0}\big):=(x_{0}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{0})

and for
n\geq 0
, defining
\big(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1},\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}\big)
as

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-\bigg(\frac{e^{-\gamma h_{n}}+\gamma h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}}\bigg)u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)+\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dW_{s}\hskip 0.7113ptdt,

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}

\displaystyle:=e^{-\gamma h_{n}}\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)+\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}e^{-\gamma(t_{n+1}-t)}dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

5
Numerical experiment

In this section, we shall empirically compare the proposed SOFA method with the other unadjusted Langevin MCMC algorithms previously discussed. In our experiment, the target density
\pi(\theta)\propto\exp(-f(\theta))
comes from a logistic regression on a real-world dataset (German credit data in
[
40
]
) where there are
m=1000
individuals, each with
d=49
features
x_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and a label
y_{i}\in\{-1,1\}
indicating whether they are creditworthy or not.
The model states that
\mathbb{P}(Y_{i}=y_{i}\hskip 0.7113pt|\hskip 0.7113ptx_{i})=\big(1+e^{-y_{i}\hskip 0.7113ptx_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\theta}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)^{-1}
and so the function
f
is defined as

f(\theta)=\frac{\delta}{2}\|\theta\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\log\hskip-0.7113pt\big(1+\exp(-y_{i}\hskip 0.7113ptx_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}\theta)\big)\hskip 0.7113pt,

where
\delta
is a regularization parameter that we set to
\delta=0.1
. We also set
\gamma=2
and
u=1
so that the SDE governing the underdamped Langevin diffusion (equation (
1.1
)) becomes

\displaystyle d\theta_{t}=v_{t}\,dt,\hskip 21.33955ptdv_{t}

\displaystyle=-2\hskip 0.7113ptv_{t}\,dt-\nabla f(\theta_{t})\,dt+2\,dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(5.1)

In the regression, the weight vector
\theta
takes values in
\mathbb{R}^{49}
. For example, this means that
W
is a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion with
d=49
. We generate
\theta_{0}
using a normal prior:

\theta_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,10I_{d}\Big)\hskip 0.7113pt.

We use the following Monte Carlo estimator as a proxy for the
\mathbb{L}_{2}
approximation error.

Definition 5.1

(
Strong error estimator
)
.

Let
T>0
be a fixed time and
\big\{\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptk}^{h\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}}\big\}_{k\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
denote a numerical solution of (
5.1
) computed at times
t_{k}:=kh
using step size
h=\frac{T}{N}
where
N\geq 1
. Similarly, let
\big\{\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptk}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\big\}_{k\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
be the approximation obtained using step size
\frac{1}{2}h
.
We generate
n
samples of these numerical solutions (at time
T
) and define the estimator

S_{N,n}:=\sqrt{\,\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptN,i}^{h\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}}-\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt2N,i}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\big\|_{2}^{2}\,}\,,

(5.2)

where each pair
\big(\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptN,i}^{h\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}}\hskip 0.7113pt,\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt2N,i}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)
is computed from the same sample path of Brownian motion
and each initial condition
\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0,i}^{h\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}}=\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0,i}^{\frac{1}{2}h}
is sampled from the normal distribution
\mathcal{N}\big(0,10\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d}\big)
.

Remark 5.2
.

By the law of large numbers, the estimator (
5.2
) converges as
n\rightarrow\infty
to

S_{N}:=\big\|\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptN}^{h\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}}-\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt2N}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,,

almost surely. For large step sizes,
S_{N}
may be not close to the true
\mathbb{L}_{2}
error
\big\|\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptN}^{h\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}}-\theta_{\hskip 0.7113ptT}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}
.

For the experiment, we use a time horizon of
T=1000
, which is greater than the mixing time of Hamiltonian Monte Carlo for this specific problem (see example 3.2 in
[
1
]
).
We compute the error estimator (
5.2
) using
n=100
independent samples of
\big(\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptN}^{h\begin{matrix}\\[-12.0pt]
\end{matrix}}\hskip 0.7113pt,\overrightarrow{\theta\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt2N}^{\frac{1}{2}h}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)
.

We will now present our results for the numerical experiment that is described above.
Code for this experiment can be found at
github.com/james-m-foster/high-order-langevin
.

Figure 5.1
:
Graph showing
S_{N,n}
computed for various numerical methods and step sizes
h=\frac{T}{N}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

From the above graph, we see that the SORT and SOFA methods are the most accurate when a sufficiently small step size is used. For example, with a step size of
h=0.005
,
the “shifted ODE” methods are roughly 50 times more accurate than the Strang splitting.
Apart from the SOFA method, all of the numerical methods exhibit the convergence rates that one would expect (see Section
4
for details). In particular, we note that the OBABO scheme can achieve second order
2
-Wasserstein convergence to the target distribution
\pi
.
However, since the estimator (
5.2
) corresponds to the
\mathbb{L}_{2}
convergence to the SDE solution, the OBABO scheme only achieves first order convergence in our numerical experiment.
Since it is difficult to estimate the
2
-Wasserstein distance between approximations and solutions of high-dimensional underdamped Langevin dynamics (see example 4.3 in
[
19
]
), we were unable to empirically show the second order convergence of the OBABO scheme.

Perhaps the most surprising aspect of Figure
5.1
is that the SOFA method appears to exhibit a fourth order convergence rate (whereas the theory only suggests a third order).
One possible explanation for this is that the constant in front of the “
\hskip 0.35565ptO(h^{3})
component” of
S_{N}
is negligible for the range of steps sizes used in the numerical experiment. That is,
the noise term in the SDE (
5.1
) has a small impact on the accuracy of the approximation (recall that the SOFA method will reduce to a fourth order splitting method when
\sigma=0
).

Overall the shifted ODE and Strang splitting methods exhibit the fastest convergence. Furthermore, the SORT and SOFA methods with a step size of
h=0.01
are comparable
to the Strang splitting with smaller step size of
h=0.025
(and are thus more efficient).
For MCMC-type applications, the OBABO scheme may also perform well since it can be Metropolis-adjusted and achieves a second order
2
-Wasserstein convergence rate when
\nabla f
,
\nabla^{2}f
are Lipschitz continuous. If the target log-density
f
only has Lipschitz gradient, then the randomized midpoint method has the optimal order of strong convergence
[
8
]
.
However, for applications where approximate Langevin MCMC is suitable and the target log-density
f
has some degree of smoothness, the theory suggests that the shifted ODE is state-of-the-art and this is supported by our experiment for the SORT and SOFA methods.

6
Conclusion

In this paper, we present an ODE approximation for underdamped Langevin dynamics
and establish non-asymptotic error estimates (in
L^{2}(\mathbb{P})
) under different assumptions on
f

which then give bounds on the
2
-Wasserstein convergence to the stationary distribution.
Most notably, we show that the “shifted ODE” method exhibits third order convergence when
\nabla f,\nabla^{2}f
and
\nabla^{3}f
are all Lipschitz continuous (that is, it achieves
W_{2}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,e^{-f}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)\leq\varepsilon
in
\mathcal{O}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}/\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)
steps). To the best of our knowledge, this is the first approximation of
ULD to achieve
1/\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}
dependence without requiring one to evaluate the higher derivatives of
f
.
By discretizing the shifted ODE using standard high order ODE solvers, we derive practical MCMC algorithms that exhibited third order convergence in our numerical experiment.
In addition to the proposed methods, we found that the Strang splitting (definition
4.2
) is a conceptually simple method that does not appear to be extensively studied in the literature yet offers an attractive compromise between accuracy and computational cost.

7
Future work

We believe the SORT method requires further analysis and can be potentially improved.
For example, by modifying the noise terms, we arrive at the following numerical method:

Definition 7.1

(
A SORT-like method with midpoint computation
)
.

Let
\{t_{n}\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
be a sequence of times with
t_{0}=0
,

t_{n+1}>t_{n}
and step sizes
h_{n}=t_{n+1}-t_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt
, for
n\geq 0
. We construct a numerical solution

\{(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\hskip 0.7113pt,\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt,\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn})\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
for the SDE (
1.1
) by setting
(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0}\hskip 0.7113pt,\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt0}):=(x_{0}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{0})

where
v_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,u\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d}\big)
and for
n\geq 0
, defining
(\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}\hskip 0.7113pt,\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt,\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1})
by

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(1)}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-\bigg(\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}}\bigg)u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\hskip 0.7113pt\left(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{3}{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-2.84526pt\bigg(\frac{e^{-\gamma(t_{n+1}-t)}+\gamma(t_{n+1}-t)-1}{\gamma^{2}h_{n}}\bigg)dW_{t}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt-\left.\frac{1}{4}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-2.84526pt\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma(t_{n+1}-t)}}{\gamma}\bigg)\,dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt\right),

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-\bigg(\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{2}{3}u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)+\frac{1}{3}u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(1)}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n}+\frac{1}{2}h_{n}}\hskip-2.84526pt\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma(t_{n}+\frac{1}{2}h_{n}-t)}}{\gamma}\bigg)\,dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}

\displaystyle:=\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma h_{n}}}{\gamma}\bigg)\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-\bigg(\frac{e^{-\gamma h_{n}}+\gamma h_{n}-1}{\gamma^{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\frac{1}{3}\hskip 0.7113ptu\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)+\frac{2}{3}\hskip 0.7113ptu\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(1)}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-2.84526pt\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma(t_{n+1}-t)}}{\gamma}\bigg)\,dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}

\displaystyle:=e^{-\gamma h_{n}}\overrightarrow{v\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}-\frac{1}{6}\hskip 0.7113pte^{-\gamma h_{n}}u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}\big)h_{n}-\frac{2}{3}\hskip 0.7113pte^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma h_{n}}u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn}^{\hskip 0.7113pt(1)}\big)h_{n}-\frac{1}{6}u\nabla f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526ptn+1}\big)h_{n}

\displaystyle\hskip 31.29802pt+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}e^{-\gamma(t_{n+1}-t)}dW_{t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Here the stochastic integrals are jointly normal and thus straightforward to generate.
Similar to the SORT method, we conjecture that this method achieves a
2
-Wasserstein error of
\varepsilon
in
\mathcal{O}\big(\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}/\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{k}}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)
steps when the first
k
derivatives of
f
are Lipschitz continuous (
k=1,2,3
). However, the key advantage of the above unadjsuted Langevin MCMC algorithm is that it produces
2N
samples
\{\overrightarrow{x\,}_{\hskip-2.84526pt\frac{1}{2}n}\}_{1\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptn\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pt2N}
using only
2N
evaluations of
\nabla f
.

Another avenue for future research would be to explore if the “shifted ODE” approach
can be applied to related SDEs, such as high-order
[
48
]
or adaptive
[
37
]
Langevin dynamics.

.

Appendix A
Preliminary theorems

We first recall the following standard results, which will be used within our error analysis.

Theorem A.1
.

For
p\geq 1
and
a_{1},\cdots,a_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg\|_{2}^{p}\leq k^{p-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|a_{i}\|_{2}^{p}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

For any
p\geq 1
, the function
g(x)=x^{p}
is convex and strictly increasing on
[0,\infty)
.
Applying the triangle inequality followed by Jensen’s inequality gives

\displaystyle g\Bigg(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{k}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg\|_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg)\leq g\Bigg(\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|a_{i}\|_{2}\Bigg)\leq\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k}g\big(\|a_{i}\|_{2}\big).

The result immediately follows from the definition of
g(x)
.
∎

Theorem A.2
.

Let
z:[s,t]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
be such that
\|z\|_{2}^{p}
is integrable on
[s,t]
for some
p\geq 1
. Then

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{r}\,dr\,\bigg\|_{2}^{p}\leq(t-s)^{p-1}\int_{s}^{t}\|z_{r}\|_{2}^{p}\,dr.

Proof.

For any
p\geq 1
, the function
g(x)=x^{p}
is convex and strictly increasing on
[0,\infty)
.
By viewing
\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}\,\cdot\,\,d\tau\hskip 0.7113pt
as an expectation with respect to the uniform measure on
[s,t]
,
we can apply Jensen’s inequality to give

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}g\big(\|z_{r}\|_{2}\big)\,dr\,\leq\,g\bigg(\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}\|z_{r}\|_{2}\,dr\bigg).

The result immediately follows from the definition of
g(x)
.
∎

Theorem A.3

(Minkowski’s inequality)
.

Let
X
and
Y
be
\mathbb{R}^{n}
-valued random variables that are
\mathbb{L}_{p}
-integrable for some
p\geq 1
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|X+Y\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\big\|X\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}+\big\|Y\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}.

(A.1)

Proof.

Setting
z:=X\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{[0,1]}+Y\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{[1,2]}
and
[s,t]=[0,2]
in Theorem
A.4
gives the result.
∎

Theorem A.4

(Minkowski’s inequality for integrals)
.

Let
z
denote a
\mathbb{R}^{n}
-valued stochastic process which is
\mathbb{L}_{p}
-integrable over an interval
[s,t]
for some
p\geq 1
. Then

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{r}\,dr\,\bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\int_{s}^{t}\|z_{r}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,dr

(A.2)

Proof.

By Theorem
A.2
, it is clear that the left hand side of (
A.2
) is finite. Moreover, when
p=1
, the result directly follows by the linearity of expectation. For
p>1
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{r}\,dr\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{\tau}\,d\tau\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{r}\,dr\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|_{2}^{p-1}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\hskip 0.7113pt\int_{s}^{t}\|z_{\tau}\|_{2}\,d\tau\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{r}\,dr\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|_{2}^{p-1}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\hskip 0.7113pt\|z_{\tau}\|_{2}\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{r}\,dr\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|_{2}^{p-1}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg]\hskip 0.7113ptd\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|z_{\tau}\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{r}\,dr\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|_{2}^{(p-1)\cdot\frac{p}{p-1}}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg]^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\hskip 0.7113ptd\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\|z_{\tau}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,d\tau\,\bigg\|\int_{s}^{t}z_{r}\,dr\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}^{1-\frac{1}{p}},

where the penultimate line comes from Hölder’s inequality. The result now follows.
∎

Remark A.5
.

We also use the Minkowski’s inequalities for the
\mathcal{F}_{n}
-conditional
\mathbb{L}_{p}
norm:

\displaystyle\|X\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}:=\mathbb{E}\big[\|X\|_{2}^{p}\,\big|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}_{n}\big]^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Theorem A.6
.

Let
Z
be a random variable in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathcal{F}
be a
\sigma
-algebra. Then for
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}[Z|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\big\|Z\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}.

Proof.

By Jensen’s inequality and the Tower law, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}[Z|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}[Z|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,]\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\|Z\|_{2}^{p}\,\big|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,\big]\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\|Z\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}=\big\|Z\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}},

which gives the desired result.
∎

Just as in
[
39
]
, we shall apply the following theorem to establish a global error estimate.

Theorem A.7
.

Suppose
X,Y,Z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
are square-integrable random variables and that
Z
is
\mathcal{F}
-measurable (where
\mathcal{F}
is some
\sigma
-algebra). Then for constants
c,h>0
, we have

\displaystyle\|X+Y\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pt\|X\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|X-\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\|Y-Z\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\frac{c}{h}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\Big(1+\frac{h}{c}\Big)\|Y\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz and Young’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle\frac{c}{h}x,y\Big\rangle\Big|\leq\Big\|\frac{c}{h}x\Big\|_{2}\|y\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\Big\|\frac{c}{h}x\Big\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|y\|_{2}^{2}\,,

for vectors
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Rearranging the above produces the below inequality:

\displaystyle\big|\langle x,y\rangle\big|\leq\frac{c}{2h}\|x\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{h}{2c}\|y\|_{2}^{2}\,.

Applying this to the random variables
\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,]
and
Y
gives

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\langle X,Y\hskip 0.7113pt\rangle\big|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\langle X-\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,],Y\hskip 0.7113pt\rangle|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{1}}+\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,],Y\hskip 0.7113pt\rangle\big]|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\langle X-\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,],Y-Z\rangle|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{1}}+\big\|\langle\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,],Y\hskip 0.7113pt\rangle\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|X-\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\big\|Y-Z\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\frac{c}{2h}\|\mathbb{E}[X|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,]\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\frac{h}{2c}\|Y\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt,

by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. The second line follows by employing the Tower law
(for the first term) and Theorem
A.6
(for the second term). Expanding
\|X+Y\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}
gives

\displaystyle\|X+Y\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|X\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\langle X,Y\rangle\big]+\|Y\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|X\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}\big[\langle X,Y\rangle\big|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,\big]\big]+\|Y\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|X\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\langle X,Y\hskip 0.7113pt\rangle\big|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathcal{F}\,\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{1}}+\|Y\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt,

and the result follows.
∎

Along with Brownian motion, we also use Brownian bridge processes in our analysis.

Definition A.8
.

Let
W
be a standard
d
-dimensional Brownian motion. Then for
n\geq 0
, we consider the Brownian bridge

\displaystyle B_{t}=W_{t}-\bigg(W_{t_{n}}+\bigg(\frac{t-t_{n}}{h_{n}}\bigg)\hskip 0.7113ptW_{t_{n},t_{n+1}}\bigg),

and define the “shifted” Brownian bridge

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{t}:=B_{t}+12K_{n}\bigg(\frac{t-t_{n}}{h_{n}}\bigg),

for
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
where
h_{n}:=t_{n+1}-t_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

We now give some basic
\mathbb{L}_{p}
estimates for these processes as well as the random variable
H_{n}+6K_{n}
which appears in the shifted ODE (and thus appears throughout our analysis).

Theorem A.9
.

Let
W,B
and
\widetilde{B}
be the processes given by definition
A.8
. Then for all
n\geq 0
and
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptB_{t_{n},t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{B}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\big\|H_{n}+6K_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle=C_{K}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

for
p\in\{2,4,8\}
where the constants
C_{B}(p)
and
C_{K}(p)
are defined as

\displaystyle C(p)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\hskip 0.7113pt1&\text{if}\,\,\,p=2\\
\hskip 0.7113pt\big(1+2d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-1}\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}&\text{if}\,\,\,p=4\\
\hskip 0.7113pt\big(1+12d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-1}+44d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-2}+48d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-3}\big)^{\frac{1}{8}}&\text{if}\,\,\,p=8\end{cases}\,,

\displaystyle C_{B}(p)

\displaystyle:=\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{5}}{5}\right)\hskip-1.42262ptC(p),

\displaystyle C_{K}(p)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sqrt{30}}{15}C(p).

(Note that
\hskip 0.7113ptC(p)\sqrt{d\,}
is the
\mathbb{L}_{p}
norm of a standard
d
-dimensional normal random variable).

Proof.

To begin, we will consider a standard
d
-dimensional normal random variable
Z
.
The
\mathbb{L}_{2}
and
\mathbb{L}_{4}
norms of
Z=\big(Z^{(1)},\cdots,Z^{(d)}\big)
are straightforward to explicitly compute.

\displaystyle\big\|Z\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k\hskip 0.7113pt=\hskip 0.7113pt0}^{d}\big(Z^{(k)}\big)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=d,

\displaystyle\big\|Z\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}^{4}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\,\sum_{k\hskip 0.7113pt=\hskip 0.7113pt0}^{d}\big(Z^{(k)}\big)^{2}\right)^{2}\,\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti,\hskip 0.7113ptj\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd\\[1.0pt]
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z^{(i)}\big)^{2}\big(Z^{(j)}\big)^{2}\Big]+\sum_{k\hskip 0.7113pt=\hskip 0.7113pt0}^{d}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z^{(k)}\big)^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle=d(d-1)+3d

\displaystyle=d^{2}+2d.

Note that we used the fact that centred normal distributions have trivial odd moments.
Similarly, one can compute the
\mathbb{L}_{8}
norm of
Z
(though the calculation is more involved).

\displaystyle\big\|Z\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{8}}^{8}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\,\sum_{k\hskip 0.7113pt=\hskip 0.7113pt0}^{d}\big(Z^{(k)}\big)^{2}\right)^{4}\,\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti,\hskip 0.7113ptj,\hskip 0.7113ptk,\hskip 0.7113ptl\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd\\[1.0pt]
|\{i,j,k,l\}|=4\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z^{(i)}\big)^{2}\big(Z^{(j)}\big)^{2}\big(Z^{(k)}\big)^{2}\big(Z^{(l)}\big)^{2}\Big]+6\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti,\hskip 0.7113ptj,\hskip 0.7113ptk\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd\\[1.0pt]
|\{i,j,k\}|=3\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z^{(i)}\big)^{4}\big(Z^{(j)}\big)^{2}\big(Z^{(k)}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+3\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti,\hskip 0.7113ptj\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd\\[1.0pt]
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z^{(i)}\big)^{4}\big(Z^{(j)}\big)^{4}\Big]+4\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113pti,\hskip 0.7113ptj\hskip 0.7113pt\leq\hskip 0.7113ptd\\[1.0pt]
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z^{(i)}\big)^{6}\big(Z^{(j)}\big)^{2}\Big]+\sum_{k\hskip 0.7113pt=\hskip 0.7113pt0}^{d}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(Z^{(k)}\big)^{8}\Big]

\displaystyle=d(d-1)(d-2)(d-3)+18d(d-1)(d-2)+27d(d-1)+60d(d-1)+105d

\displaystyle=d^{4}+12d^{3}+44d^{2}+48d.

Since the Brownian bridge on
[0,1]
is a Gaussian process with covariance function

K_{B}(s,t)=\min(s,t)-st
, it is clear that a rescaled bridge process on
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
has variance

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\big(B_{t_{n},t}\big)=\frac{(t_{n+1}-t)(t-t_{n})}{h_{n}}\hskip 0.7113pt,

for
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
. Hence for
p\in\{2,4,8\}
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptB_{t_{n},t}\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}=\bigg(\frac{(t_{n+1}-t)(t-t_{n})}{h_{n}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|Z\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq C(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

for
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
. Finally, since
H_{n}
and
K_{n}
are independent normal random variables with

\displaystyle H_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}\bigg(0,\frac{1}{12}h_{n}\bigg),\hskip 14.22636ptK_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}\bigg(0,\frac{1}{720}h_{n}\bigg),

we have
\|H_{n}+6K_{n}\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}=\frac{\sqrt{30}}{15}(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|Z\hskip 0.7113pt\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}
for
n\geq 0
and the result follows.
∎

Theorem A.10
.

For
n\geq 0
, the triple iterated integral of the shifted Brownian bridge on
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\widetilde{B}_{t_{k},s}\,ds\,dt.

Proof.

The result follows by using integration by parts with the definitions of
H_{n}
and
K_{n}
.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}h_{n}^{2}H_{n}+3h_{n}^{2}K_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}h_{n}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}B_{t_{n},t}\,dt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}h_{n}-(t-t_{n})\bigg)B_{t_{n},t}\,dt+2h_{n}^{2}K_{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(h_{n}-(t-t_{n})\big)B_{t_{n},t}\,dt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}12K_{n}\bigg(\frac{s-t_{n}}{h_{n}}\bigg)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\,\big(h_{n}-(t-t_{n})\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t}B_{t_{n},s}\,ds\,\Bigg]_{t\hskip 0.7113pt=\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n}}^{t\hskip 0.7113pt=\hskip 0.7113ptt_{n+1}}-\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}-\int_{t_{n}}^{t}B_{t_{n},s}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}12K_{n}\bigg(\frac{s-t_{n}}{h_{n}}\bigg)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\bigg(B_{t_{k},s}+12K_{n}\bigg(\frac{s-t_{n}}{h_{n}}\bigg)\bigg)\,ds\,dt
.
∎

Recall that each step of the shifted ODE approximation (over
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
) is given by

\displaystyle\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,x_{n+1}^{\prime}\\[-3.0pt]
\,v_{n+1}^{\prime}\end{matrix}\Bigg):=\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t_{n+1}}\\[-3.0pt]
\,\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t_{n+1}}\end{matrix}\Bigg)+12K_{n}\Bigg(\begin{matrix}0\\[-3.0pt]
\sigma\end{matrix}\,\Bigg),

where
\big\{\big(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t},\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big)\big\}_{t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]}
solves the following ODE,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\\[-3.0pt]
\,\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\end{matrix}\Bigg)=\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\\[-3.0pt]
\,-\gamma\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)-u\nabla f\big(\,\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big)\end{matrix}\,\Bigg)+\frac{W_{n}-12K_{n}}{h_{n}}\,\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,0\\[-3.0pt]
\,\sigma\end{matrix}\,\Bigg)\hskip 0.7113pt,

with initial condition
\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t_{n}}\hskip 0.7113pt,\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t_{n}}\big)\hskip 0.7113pt:=\hskip 0.7113pt\big(x_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{n}\big)
. In addition, we will set
(\hskip 0.7113ptx_{0}^{\prime}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{0}^{\prime}\hskip 0.7113pt)\hskip 0.7113pt:=\hskip 0.7113pt(x_{0}\hskip 0.7113pt,v_{0})
. We shall now present some Taylor expansions for the above ODE and the true diffusion.

Theorem A.11
.

For
n\geq 0
, the integral of
\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-v\big)
over
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}\big)\,dt+\sigma h_{n}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)

(A.3)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\gamma^{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big)\,dr\,ds\,dt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma(h_{n})^{3}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)+\gamma u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{r})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})\big)\,dr\,ds\,dt.

Proof.

Since
\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}
and
v
both satisfy differential equations,
(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t})
can be expanded as

\displaystyle\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}

\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big)\,ds+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big)\,ds

(A.4)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\sigma B_{t_{n},t}-\gamma\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)(t-t_{n})-12\sigma K_{n}\bigg(\frac{t-t_{n}}{h_{n}}\bigg)\,.

Hence, by integrating the above expression of
\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}
over the interval
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}\big)\,dt

\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big)\,ds\,dt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\sigma h_{n}H_{n}-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\sigma h_{n}^{2}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)-6\sigma h_{n}K_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt.

So by Theorem
A.10
, we can express the final term using a triple iterated integral of
B
.

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}\big)\,dt+\sigma h_{n}H_{n}+\sigma h_{n}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big)\,ds\,dt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\gamma\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}B_{t_{k},s}\,ds\,dt-2\gamma\sigma h_{n}^{2}K_{n}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\bigg(v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-\sigma B_{t_{n},s}-12\sigma K_{n}\bigg(\frac{s-t_{n}}{h_{n}}\bigg)\bigg)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big)\,ds\,dt.

The result follows by expanding the integrand of the first iterated integral using (
A.4
).
∎

Similarly, we present an expansion for the integrals involving the position components.

Theorem A.12
.

For
n\geq 0
, we can write the integral of
\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})-\nabla f(x)
over
[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
as

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt

(A.5)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})\big)\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s})\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\sigma\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},s}\Big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\sigma\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\,ds\,dt.

Proof.

By Itô’s lemma and the standard chain rule, we have that for
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
,

\displaystyle\nabla f(x_{t})=\nabla f(x_{t_{n}})+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})\,dx_{s}\hskip 11.38109pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})=\nabla f(x_{t_{n}})+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Since
\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}
and
x
satisfy differential equations, we can further expand the above integrals.

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\Bigg(\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})\,dx_{s}\Bigg)\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\Big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\Big)-\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})\hskip 0.7113ptv_{s}\,ds\,dt.

By adding and subtracting integrals involving
\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})
, we obtain the following formula:

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})\big)\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\sigma\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\sigma\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\,ds\,dt.

Evaluating the final iterated integral and applying Theorem
A.10
gives

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\sigma\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sigma h_{n}^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)

\displaystyle=\sigma\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\widetilde{B}_{t_{k},s}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\sigma\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\widetilde{B}_{t_{k},s}\,ds\,dt.

The result follows by plugging this into the formula for the integral of
\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})-\nabla f(x)
.
∎

If we assume that the third derivative of
f
exists, we can use the following expansion:

Theorem A.13
.

For
n\geq 0
and
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, we can Taylor expand
\nabla f(x_{t})
and
\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})
as

\displaystyle\nabla f(x_{t})

\displaystyle=\nabla f(x_{t_{n}})+\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(x_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big)+\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(x_{s}-x_{t_{n}}\big)\,dx_{s}

(A.6)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{r})-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\,dx_{r}\,dx_{s}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})

\displaystyle=\nabla f(x_{t_{n}})+\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big)+\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-x_{t_{n}}\big)\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}

(A.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

The result is a consequence of Itô’s lemma and the integration of parts formula.
∎

As one might expect, Theorems
A.12
and
A.13
will respectively lead to useful
\mathbb{L}_{2}
estimates after we impose Lipschitz regularity on the Hessian and third derivative of
f
.
Without these additional assumptions, we shall apply Lipschitz estimates directly to (
A.3
).

Appendix B
Dimension-free estimates for the shifted ODE

In order to establish the convergence rates given in Table
1.1
, we shall prove a contractivity
result for the shifted ODE using the arguments given by the proof of Proposition 1 in
[
16
]
.
Recall that we define the shifted ODE approximation
\big\{\big(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
for the SDE (
1.1
) as

\displaystyle\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,\widetilde{x}_{n+1}\\[-3.0pt]
\,\widetilde{v}_{n+1}\end{matrix}\Bigg):=\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,\widehat{x}_{t_{n+1}}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\\[-3.0pt]
\,\widehat{v}_{t_{n+1}}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\end{matrix}\Bigg)+12K_{n}\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,0\\[-3.0pt]
\,\sigma\end{matrix}\,\Bigg),

where
\big\{\big(\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn},\widehat{v}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big)\big\}_{t\hskip 0.7113pt\in\hskip 0.7113pt[t_{n},t_{n+1}]}
solves the following ODE,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,\widehat{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\\[-3.0pt]
\,\widehat{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\end{matrix}\Bigg)=\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,\widehat{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\\[-3.0pt]
\,-\gamma\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)-u\nabla f\big(\,\widehat{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big)\end{matrix}\,\Bigg)+\frac{W_{n}-12K_{n}}{h_{n}}\,\Bigg(\begin{matrix}\,0\\[-3.0pt]
\,\sigma\end{matrix}\,\Bigg)\hskip 0.7113pt,

with initial condition
\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{x}_{t_{n}}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn},\widehat{v}_{t_{n}}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big):=\big(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)
.

Theorem B.1

(Exponential contractivity of the shifted ODE)
.

For each
n\geq 0
, we define

\displaystyle y_{n}

\displaystyle:=\begin{pmatrix}\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\\
\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\end{pmatrix},\hskip 14.22636pt\widetilde{y}_{n}:=\begin{pmatrix}\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{n}^{\prime}+v_{n}^{\prime}\big)\\
\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113ptx_{n}^{\prime}+v_{n}^{\prime}\big)\end{pmatrix},

where
\lambda\in[0,\frac{1}{2}\gamma)
and
\eta:=\gamma-\lambda
. Suppose
f
is
m
-strongly convex and
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz. Then

\displaystyle\big\|\widetilde{y}_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\leq e^{-\alpha h_{n}}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(B.1)

where the constant
\alpha
is given by

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle:=\frac{(\eta^{2}-uM)\vee(um-\lambda^{2})}{\gamma-2\lambda}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

We will follow the same arguments that were used to establish Proposition 1 in
[
16
]
.
We first consider the following random variables:

\displaystyle w_{t}^{n}:=\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}+\widehat{v}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}+\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big),

\displaystyle z_{t}^{n}:=\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}+\widehat{v}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big)-\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}+\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big).

Note that
f
is twice differentiable, so we can apply Taylor’s theorem to give

\displaystyle\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}f\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}+r(\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})\big)\,dr}_{=:\,\mathcal{H}_{t}^{n}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big).

Using this formula with the ODEs for
(\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn},\widehat{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})
and
(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn},\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})
, the derivatives of
w^{n}
and
z^{n}
become

\displaystyle\frac{dw^{n}}{dt}

\displaystyle=(\gamma-\lambda)\hskip 0.7113pt\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-\widehat{v}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big)+u\hskip 0.7113pt\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})\big)

\displaystyle=\eta\,\frac{\lambda z_{t}^{n}-\eta w_{t}^{n}}{\eta-\lambda}+u\mathcal{H}_{t}\,\frac{w_{t}^{n}-z_{t}^{n}}{\eta-\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{\big(u\mathcal{H}_{t}^{n}-\eta^{2}I_{d}\big)w_{t}^{n}+\big(\eta\lambda I_{d}-u\mathcal{H}_{t}\big)z_{t}^{n}}{\eta-\lambda}\,,

and

\displaystyle\frac{dz^{n}}{dt}

\displaystyle=(\gamma-\eta)\hskip 0.7113pt\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-\widehat{v}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}\big)+u\hskip 0.7113pt\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})\big)

\displaystyle=\lambda\,\frac{\lambda z_{t}^{n}-\eta w_{t}^{n}}{\eta-\lambda}+u\mathcal{H}_{t}^{n}\,\frac{w_{t}^{n}-z_{t}^{n}}{\eta-\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{\big(u\mathcal{H}_{t}^{n}-\eta\lambda I_{d}\big)w_{t}^{n}-\big(u\mathcal{H}_{t}^{n}-\lambda^{2}I_{d}\big)z_{t}^{n}}{\eta-\lambda}\,,

where
I_{d}
is the
d\times d
identity matrix. Note that we have used the fact that
\eta+\lambda=\gamma
.
Using these formulae along with the chain rule, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Big(\|w_{t}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\|z_{t}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=2\hskip 0.7113pt(w_{t}^{n})^{T}\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{dw_{t}^{n}}{dt}+2\hskip 0.7113pt(z_{t}^{n})^{T}\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{dz_{t}^{n}}{dt}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\eta-\lambda}\Big((w_{t}^{n})^{T}\big(u\mathcal{H}_{t}^{n}-\eta^{2}I_{d}\big)(w_{t}^{n})-(z_{t}^{n})^{T}\big(u\mathcal{H}_{t}^{n}-\lambda^{2}I_{d}\big)(z_{t}^{n})\Big).

Since
f
is
m
-strongly convex and its gradient
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz continuous, we have
mI_{d}\preccurlyeq\nabla^{2}f(x)\preccurlyeq MI_{d}

for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. In particular, this implies
mI_{d}\preccurlyeq\mathcal{H}_{t}\preccurlyeq MI_{d}
and hence

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Big(\|w_{t}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\|z_{t}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\eta-\lambda}\Big(\big(uM-\eta^{2}\big)\|w_{t}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\big(\lambda^{2}-um\big)\|z_{t}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\big((uM-\eta^{2})\vee(\lambda^{2}-um)\big)}{\eta-\lambda}\Big(\|w_{t}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\|z_{t}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\Big).

Therefore by Grönwall’s inequality (Corollary 3 in
[
20
]
), we have that

\displaystyle\|w_{t_{n+1}}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\|z_{t_{n+1}}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\exp\hskip-0.7113pt\left(-\frac{2\big((\eta^{2}-uM)\vee(um-\lambda^{2})\big)}{\eta-\lambda}\,h_{n}\right)\hskip-0.7113pt\Big(\|w_{t_{n}}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\|z_{t_{n}}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\Big).

The result now follows as
\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n+1}-x_{n+1}^{\prime}=\widehat{x}_{t_{n+1}}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t_{n+1}}
and
\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{v}_{n+1}-v_{n+1}^{\prime}=\widehat{v}_{t_{n+1}}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t_{n+1}}
.
∎

Remark B.2
.

Any contraction theorem for ULD whose proof uses a synchronous coupling of Brownian motions is likely to hold for the shifted ODE as its driven by a linear path.

Remark B.3
.

Provided that
\gamma^{2}>uM
, the contraction rate
\alpha
becomes positive if
\lambda=0
.

In addition to the above theorem, we will prove some further dimension-free estimates.

Theorem B.4
.

Suppose the gradient
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz. Then for each
n\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{y}_{n+1}-y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\leq C_{1}h_{n}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(B.2)

where the constant
C_{1}
is given by

\displaystyle C_{1}:=\frac{\sqrt{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\eta}{\eta-\lambda}\exp\bigg(\gamma h_{n}+\frac{1}{2}uMh_{n}^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\gamma+uMh_{n}\bigg).

Proof.

As
(\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn},\widehat{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})
and
(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn},\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})
satisfy ODEs, the
M
-Lipschitz regularity of
\nabla f
gives

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{s}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq(t-t_{n})\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{v}_{n}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{2}+\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{r}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\widehat{x}_{r}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\leq(t-t_{n})\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{v}_{n}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{2}+\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{r}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+uM\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\int_{t_{n}}^{r}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{w}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{w}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,dw\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\leq(t-t_{n})\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{v}_{n}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\gamma+uM(s-t_{k})\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{r}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,dr\,ds\hskip 0.7113pt.

It then follows from Gronwall’s inequality (Corollary 2 in
[
20
]
) that

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{s}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,ds\leq\exp\bigg(\gamma h_{n}+\frac{1}{2}uMh_{n}^{2}\bigg)\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{v}_{n}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt.

The first component of
\widetilde{y}_{n+1}-y_{n}
can be estimated as

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n+1}+\widetilde{v}_{n+1}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{n+1}^{\prime}+v_{n+1}^{\prime}\big)\Big)-\Big(\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\Big)\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\Big\|\Big(\big(\lambda\big(\widehat{x}_{t_{n+1}}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\widetilde{x}_{n}\big)+\big(\widehat{v}_{t_{n+1}}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)\big)-\big(\lambda\big(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t_{n+1}}-x_{t_{n}}\big)+\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t_{n+1}}-v_{t_{n}}\big)\big)\Big)\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\bigg\|\big(\lambda-\gamma\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big)\,dt-u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\widehat{x}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})\big)\,dt\,\bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{t}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,dt+uM\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widehat{v}_{s}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\exp\bigg(\gamma h_{n}+\frac{1}{2}uMh_{n}^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\hskip 0.7113pth_{n}+\frac{1}{2}uMh_{n}^{2}\bigg)\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{v}_{n}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt.

The above holds for the second component of
\widetilde{y}_{n+1}-y_{n}
(simply by replacing
\lambda
with
\eta
).
So by the triangle inequality and the fact that
\lambda+\eta=\gamma
, we have the following inequality

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{y}_{n+1}-y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\leq\exp\bigg(\gamma h_{n}+\frac{1}{2}uMh_{n}^{2}\bigg)\bigg(\gamma+uMh_{n}\bigg)h_{n}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{v}_{n}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt.

The result immediately follows as

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{v}_{n}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta-\lambda}\bigg\|\hskip 0.7113pt\eta\Big(\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\Big)-\lambda\Big(\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\Big)\bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\eta-\lambda}\Big(\big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\big\|_{2}+\big\|\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\big\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\eta}{\eta-\lambda}\Big(\big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}+\big\|\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{n}+\widetilde{v}_{n}\big)-\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the final line is a direct consequence of Theorem
A.1
.
∎

Theorem B.5
.

Suppose that
f
is
m
-strongly convex and its gradient
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz. For
n\geq 0
, we define

\displaystyle y_{n}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\begin{pmatrix}\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{n}^{\prime}+v_{n}^{\prime}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\\
\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113ptx_{n}^{\prime}+v_{n}^{\prime}\big)-\big(\eta\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n}}+v_{t_{n}}\big)\end{pmatrix},

where
\lambda\in[0,\frac{1}{2}\gamma)
and
\eta:=\gamma-\lambda
are the constants that were used in Theorem
B.1
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\hskip 0.7113ptC_{1}h_{n}\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{{\mathbb{L}_{2}}}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

(B.3)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{c}{h_{n}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\Big(1+\frac{1}{c}h_{n}\Big)e^{-2\alpha h_{n}}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2},

for any
c>0
.

Proof.

By setting
X=y_{n+1}^{\prime}\hskip 0.7113pt,Y=\widetilde{y}_{n+1}\hskip 0.7113pt,Z=y_{n}
and
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{t_{n}}
in Theorem
A.7
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}+\widetilde{y}_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\big\|\widetilde{y}_{n+1}-y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{c}{h_{n}}\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\Big(1+\frac{1}{c}h_{n}\Big)\big\|\widetilde{y}_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

Note that
y_{n+1}=y_{n+1}^{\prime}+\widetilde{y}_{n+1}\hskip 0.7113pt
, and by the Tower law we can estimate the second term as

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}-2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle y_{n+1}^{\prime},\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}-2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\big\langle y_{n+1}^{\prime},\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\rangle\big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}-\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

The result now immediately follows by Theorems
B.1
and
B.4
.
∎

Appendix C
Estimates for shifted ODE under standard assumptions

To begin this section, we will establish some local
\mathbb{L}_{p}
error estimates for the shifted ODE that only require the gradient
\nabla f
to be
M
-Lipschitz. Our bounds grow linearly with
\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}
.

Theorem C.1
.

Suppose that
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz. Then for all
n\geq 0
and
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{3}{2}},

(C.1)

\displaystyle\big\|x_{t}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n}),

(C.2)

\displaystyle\big\|v_{t}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

(C.3)

for
p\in\{2,4,8\}
where the constants
C_{2}(p),C_{3}(p)
and
C_{4}(p)
are given by

\displaystyle C_{2}(p)

\displaystyle:=\sigma\bigg(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{2}{3}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{B}(p)+\frac{1}{2}\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(p)h_{n}+\frac{1}{6}uMC_{K}(p)h_{n}^{2}\bigg)\exp\bigg(\gamma h_{n}+\frac{1}{2}uMh_{n}^{2}\bigg),

\displaystyle C_{3}(p)

\displaystyle:=C_{2}(p)+\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(p),

\displaystyle C_{4}(p)

\displaystyle:=C_{B}(p)+C_{3}(p)\Big(\gamma+\frac{1}{2}uMh_{n}\Big)h_{n}.

Proof.

By Theorems
A.3
and
A.4
along with the
M
-Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla f
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\gamma\hskip 0.7113pt\Big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{r}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\Big)\,dr+\int_{t_{n}}^{s}u\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})-\nabla f(x_{r})\big)\,dr+\sigma\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},s}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}ds

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,dr\,ds+uM\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptx_{r}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,dr\,ds+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\gamma\hskip 0.7113pt\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)(s-t_{n})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,dr\,ds+uM\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\int_{t_{n}}^{r}\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{w}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{w}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,dw\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{2}{3}\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{B}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{3}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma\hskip 0.7113pt\sigma C_{K}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\Big(\gamma+uM(s-t_{n})\Big)\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\bigg(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{2}{3}\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{B}(p)+\frac{1}{2}\gamma\hskip 0.7113pt\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(p)h_{n}+\frac{1}{6}\sigma uMC_{K}(p)h_{n}^{2}\bigg)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Therefore by Gronwall’s inequality (Corollary 2 in
[
20
]
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\bigg(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{2}{3}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{B}(p)+\frac{1}{2}\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(p)h_{n}+\frac{1}{6}uMC_{K}(p)h_{n}^{2}\bigg)\exp\bigg(\gamma h_{n}+\frac{1}{2}uMh_{n}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Using the above inequality, it is now straightforward to estimate the difference
(x_{t}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})
.

\displaystyle\big\|x_{t}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|v_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds+\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|H_{n}+6K_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\Big(C_{2}(p)+\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(p)\Big)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n}).

Finally, we can derive a similar estimate for the velocity components as

\displaystyle\big\|v_{t}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\gamma\hskip 0.7113pt\Big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\Big)\,ds+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}u\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})-\nabla f(x_{s})\big)\,ds+\sigma\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},t}\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds+uM\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-x_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds+\sigma\big\|\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.5867pt\leq\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{3}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})+\frac{1}{2}uMC_{3}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{2}+C_{B}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

Remark C.2
.

By applying the same arguments, we see that Theorem
C.1
still holds when we use the (unconditional)
\mathbb{L}_{p}
norm as the proof only required Minkowski’s inequalities.

Remark C.3
.

A particularly nice feature of these error estimates is that they are uniform.
That is, the estimates (
C.1
), (
C.2
) and (
C.3
) are independent of the initial value
\big(x_{t_{n}},v_{t_{n}}\big)
.

Theorem C.4
.

Suppose that
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz. Then for all
n\geq 0
and
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[v_{t}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{5}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{2},

(C.4)

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[x_{t}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{5}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{3},

(C.5)

for
p\in\{2,4,8\}
where the constant
C_{5}(p)
is defined as

\displaystyle C_{5}(p):=uM\hskip 0.7113ptC_{3}(p)\exp\big(\gamma h_{n}\big).

Proof.

Since
W
is a centered Gaussian process and
W_{n},H_{n},K_{n}
all have zero expectation,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113ptv_{s}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big]\,ds+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big]\,ds\bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds+uM\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptx_{s}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds+uM\hskip 0.7113ptC_{3}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{2}.

By Gronwall’s inequality (Corollary 2 in
[
20
]
), we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq uM\hskip 0.7113ptC_{3}(p)\exp\big(\gamma h_{n}\big)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{2}.

Using the above, we can estimate the position component as

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{5}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{3}.

∎

Due to the exponential contracitivty of the shifted ODE detailed by Theorem
B.1
,
we can use Theorem
C.1
to estimate the process
\big\{\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\big\}_{n\geq 0}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

Theorem C.5
.

Suppose that
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz. Then for
n\geq 0
and
\lambda\in[0,\gamma]
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\leq C_{6}(p,\lambda)\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{5}{2}}

(C.6)

for
p\in\{2,4,8\}
where the constant
C_{6}(p,\lambda)
is given by

\displaystyle C_{6}(p,\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}uMC_{3}(p)+\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\bigg(\bigg(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{4}{35}\hskip 0.7113pt\gamma^{2}+\frac{1}{6}uM\bigg)C_{2}(p)+\frac{1}{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\gamma^{2}\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(p)\bigg)h_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{24}\gamma uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{3}(p)\hskip 0.7113pth_{n}^{2}.

Proof.

By Theorem
A.11
and the definition of the shifted ODE, we have the expansion:

\displaystyle\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)

(C.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\Bigg(\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}\big)\,dt+\sigma h_{n}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\Bigg)+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\Bigg(\gamma^{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big)\,dr\,ds\,dt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 75.3998pt+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma(h_{n})^{3}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)+\gamma u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{r})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})\big)\,dr\,ds\,dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt.

Using the
M
-Lipschitz regularity of
\nabla f
and Theorem
A.4
, we can estimate these integrals.

\displaystyle\Big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq uM\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,dt+\gamma^{2}\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,dr\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|x_{s}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,ds\,dt+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)(h_{n})^{3}\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|H_{n}+6K_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\gamma uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|x_{r}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}^{n}}\,dr\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{1}{2}uMC_{3}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{5}{2}}+\frac{4}{35}\gamma^{2}\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{2}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}+\frac{1}{24}\gamma uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{3}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{9}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{6}uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{2}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}},

where the last line follows directly from the estimates (
C.1
) and (
C.2
) in Theorem
C.1
.
∎

Theorem C.6
.

Let
\big\{y_{n}^{\prime}\big\}
be the error process defined in Theorem
B.5
. Suppose that
f
is
m
-strongly convex and its gradient
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz continuous. Then for all
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}^{n}}\leq C_{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{5}{2}},

(C.8)

where the constant
C_{6}
is given by

\displaystyle C_{6}:=uMC_{3}(2)+\frac{1}{6}\gamma\bigg(\big(\gamma^{2}+uM\big)C_{2}(2)+\gamma^{2}\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(2)+\frac{1}{4}\gamma uMC_{3}(2)h_{n}\bigg)h_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Minkowski’s inequality and Theorem
C.5
.
∎

Remark C.7
.

By applying the same arguments, we see that Theorems
C.1
,
C.5
and
C.6
still hold when we use the (unconditional)
\mathbb{L}_{p}
norm. This will be utilized in several proofs.

Using the above result, we obtain our first global error estimate for the shifted ODE.

Theorem C.8

(Global error estimate for the shifted ODE under standard assumptions)
.

Suppose the approximation
\big\{(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n})\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
was obtained using a constant step size
h>0
.
Let
\big\{y_{n}\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
be the error process between
(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x},\widetilde{v}\hskip 0.7113pt)
and the true diffusion
(x,v)
defined in Theorem
B.1
.
Suppose
f
is
m
-strongly convex and its gradient
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz. Then

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\frac{1-e^{-n\alpha h}}{1-e^{-\alpha h}}\,C_{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{5}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(C.9)

for
n\geq 0
, where the contraction rate
\alpha
is given by

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{\big((\gamma-\lambda)^{2}-uM\big)\vee\big(um-\lambda^{2}\big)}{\gamma-2\lambda}\,.

Proof.

Using the processes
\big(\big\{y_{n}\big\}
,
\big\{\widetilde{y}_{n}\big\}
,
\big\{y_{n}^{\prime}\big\}\big)
defined in Theorems
B.1
and
B.5
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|y_{k+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|y_{k+1}-y_{k+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\big\|y_{k+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\big\|\widetilde{y}_{k+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\big\|y_{k+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha h}\big\|y_{k}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+C_{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{5}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt,

for
k\geq 0
, where the final line is the direct consequence of Theorems
B.1
and
C.6
. Therefore

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha h}\big\|y_{n-1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+C_{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{5}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69054pt\vdots
