\displaystyle\leq e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\Big(e^{-k\alpha h}C_{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big),

and the result follows.
∎

Corollary C.9
.

Suppose that the underdamped Langevin diffusion
\big\{(x_{t},v_{t})\big\}_{t\geq 0}
and the shifted ODE approximation
\big\{(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n})\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
have the same initial velocity. Then for
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq\sqrt{\lambda^{2}+(\gamma-\lambda)^{2}}\,e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{\alpha}+h\Big)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(C.10)

Proof.

Applying the inequalities
e^{-n\alpha h}\geq 0
and
\alpha h\leq(1+\alpha h)(1-e^{-\alpha h})
to (
C.9
) yields

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{\alpha}+h\Big)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Since we assume that
\widetilde{v}_{0}=v_{0}
(which is achievable in practice as
v_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,u\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d})
), we have

\displaystyle\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{0}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{0}+v_{0}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|\big((\gamma-\lambda)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{0}\big)-\big((\gamma-\lambda)\hskip 0.7113ptx_{0}+v_{0}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt(\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|(\gamma-\lambda)(\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\lambda^{2}+(\gamma-\lambda)^{2}\Big)\big\|\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2},

and the result follows.
∎

Remark C.10
.

The above estimates holds if we set
K_{n}=0
in the definition of
\big\{(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n})\big\}
.
Moreover, removing the
K_{n}
random variable in the definition of the shifted ODE leads to the log-ODE method proposed by Castell and Gaines in
[
9
]
and recently studied in
[
25
]
.

In order to derive higher order estimates in cases where
f
has additional smoothness,
we shall first present some global bounds for both the diffusion and shifted ODE processes.
It will be helpful to assume the diffusion starts at its invariant measure (i.e.
(x_{0},v_{0})\sim\pi
).

Theorem C.11

(Global energy bounds for the ULD and shifted ODE)
.

Suppose that
f
is continuously differentiable,
m
-strongly convex and with an
M
-Lipschitz gradient
\nabla f
.
Consider an underdamped Langevin diffusion
(x,v)
with its initial value
(x_{0},v_{0})\sim\pi
,
where
\pi
denotes the stationary distribution. Then for
n\geq 0
, we have the following bounds:

\displaystyle\big\|v_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle=C_{|v|}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(C.11)

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{|\wideparen{v}|}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(C.12)

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla f(x_{t})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{Md\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(C.13)

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla f(x_{t})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt[4]{3}\,\sqrt{Md\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(C.14)

for
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
and
p\in\{2,4,8\}
, where the constants
C_{|v|}(p)
and
C_{|\wideparen{v}|}(p)
are defined as

\displaystyle C_{|v|}(p)

\displaystyle:=C(p)\sqrt{u\hskip 0.7113pt},

\displaystyle C_{|\wideparen{v}|}(p)

\displaystyle:=C_{|v|}(p)+C_{4}(p)\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

The density function for the stationary distribution
\pi
can be explicitly written as

\displaystyle\pi(x,v)\propto\exp\bigg(-f(x)-\frac{1}{2u}\|v\|_{2}^{2}\bigg).

Thus
v_{t}\,\nicefrac{{}}{{}}\sqrt{u}
is a standard
d
-dimensional normal random variable and by Theorem
A.9
,
the first result (
C.11
) follows. The second inequality can then be obtained using Minkowski’s inequality and (
C.3
) in Theorem
C.1
.
The estimate (
C.13
) is given by Lemma 2 in
[
15
]
.
In addition, we shall adapt the proof of Lemma 2 in
[
15
]
to establish the estimate (
C.14
).
Thus, we will first consider the case when
d=1
. Note that we already have the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{2}\,\pi(x)\,dx\leq M,

where
\pi(x)\propto\exp(-f(x))
is the stationary measure for the position component of ULD.
Therefore, we can define the unnormalized density function:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}(x):=\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{2}\pi(x)\hskip 0.7113pt.

Since
f
is
M
-Lipschitz continuous, it is differentiable almost everywhere and
|f^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq M
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
where the second derivative exists. By the fundamental theorem of calculus, (Theorem 7.20 in
[
51
]
), we have

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(x)-f^{\prime}(0)=\int_{0}^{x}f^{\prime\prime}(y)\,dy,

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. It is also worth noting that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dx}\Big(\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{2}\pi(x)\Big)=2f^{\prime}(x)f^{\prime\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)-\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{3}\pi^{\prime}(x),

which implies that

\displaystyle f^{\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{\pi}(x)=2f^{\prime}(x)f^{\prime\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)-\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x).

Following the proof of Lemma 2 in
[
15
]
(but using
\widetilde{\pi}
instead of
\pi
), we have the calculation:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{4}\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}(x)\big(f^{\prime}(x)\widetilde{\pi}(x)\big)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}(x)\big(2f^{\prime}(x)f^{\prime\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)-\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)\big)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{2}f^{\prime\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime}(0)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)-f^{\prime}(0)\big)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{2}f^{\prime\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx-f^{\prime}(0)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{x}f^{\prime\prime}(y)\,dy\bigg)\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)\,dx\hskip 0.7113pt.

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\pi}(x)\,dx\leq M
, we have
\widetilde{\pi}(x)\rightarrow 0
as
x\rightarrow\pm\infty
. In particular, this means that the second term
f^{\prime}(0)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)\,dx=f^{\prime}(0)\int_{\mathbb{R}}d\widetilde{\pi}
will be zero. Rewriting the last integral gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{4}\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{2}f^{\prime\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{0}^{x}f^{\prime\prime}(y)\,dy\bigg)\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)\,dx+\int_{-\infty}^{0}\bigg(\int_{x}^{0}f^{\prime\prime}(y)\,dy\bigg)\widetilde{\pi}^{\hskip 0.7113pt\prime}(x)\,dx.

Therefore, it follows from Fubini’s theorem that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{4}\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{2}f^{\prime\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx+\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{\prime\prime}(y)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{\pi}(y)\,dy+\int_{-\infty}^{0}f^{\prime\prime}(y)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{\pi}(y)\,dy.

As the integral of
\widetilde{\pi}=(f^{\prime})^{2}\pi
is bounded by
M
and
|f^{\prime\prime}|\leq M
almost everywhere, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{4}\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg|\leq 2\,\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big(f^{\prime}(x)\big)^{2}f^{\prime\prime}(x)\hskip 0.7113pt\pi(x)\,dx\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg|+\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime\prime}(y)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{\pi}(y)\,dy\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg|\leq 3M^{2}.

In the
d
-dimensional setting, we can apply this bound to each partial derivative of
f

since every marginal distribution of
\pi
will have the form
\exp(-g(x_{i}))
where
g
is
M
-Lipschitz.
Hence by Theorem
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\nabla f(x)\big\|_{2}^{4}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\bigg|\frac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\bigg|^{2}\Bigg)^{2}\,\Bigg]\leq d\,\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{d}\bigg|\frac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\bigg|^{4}\Bigg]=d\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{\partial f}{\partial x_{i}}\bigg|^{4}\Bigg]\leq 3\hskip 0.7113ptd^{2}M^{2},

where
x\sim\pi
.
∎

In the next section, we shall often compare the diffusion and shifted ODE processes at times
t
and
t_{n}
(with
t\neq t_{n}
). As a result, we will require
\mathbb{L}_{p}
estimates for such quantities.

Theorem C.12

(Local growth estimates for diffusion and ODE approximation processes)
.

Let
n\geq 0
and
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
. Then under the same assumptions as Theorem
C.11
,
we have

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{x}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}(t-t_{n}),

(C.15)

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\wideparen{x}}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n}),

(C.16)

for
p\in\{2,4,8\}
and

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{t}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{v}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

(C.17)

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\wideparen{v}}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

(C.18)

for
p\in\{2,4\}
where the constants
C_{x}(p)\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{x}}(p)\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptC_{v}(p)\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{v}}(p)
are given by

\displaystyle C_{x}(p)

\displaystyle:=C(p)\sqrt{u\hskip 0.7113pt},

\displaystyle C_{\wideparen{x}}(p)

\displaystyle:=C_{x}(p)+C_{3}(p)(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle C_{v}(p)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC(2)+\Big(\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|v|}(2)+u\sqrt{M\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big)(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}&\text{if}\,\,\,p=2\\[3.0pt]
\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC(4)+\Big(\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|v|}(4)+\sqrt[4]{3}\hskip 0.7113ptu\sqrt{M\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt\Big)(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}&\text{if}\,\,\,p=4\end{cases}\,,

\displaystyle C_{\wideparen{v}}(p)

\displaystyle:=C_{v}(p)+C_{4}(p).

Proof.

The first inequality (
C.15
) directly follows from the global energy bound (
C.11
).

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\,ds\leq C(p)\sqrt{ud\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n}).

By applying Minkowski’s inequality and the local estimate (
C.2
) to the above, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}+\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq C(p)\sqrt{ud\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})+C_{3}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n}),

which is the second inequality (
C.16
). Similarly the bounds (
C.11
), (
C.13
) and (
C.14
) give

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{t}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds+\sigma\big\|W_{t_{n},t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|v|}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})+u\sqrt{Md\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})+\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{t}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,ds+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,ds+\sigma\big\|W_{t_{n},t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|v|}(4)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})+\sqrt[4]{3}\hskip 0.7113ptu\sqrt{Md\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})+\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC(4)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

and the third inequality (
C.17
) follows. Applying Minkowski’s inequality as before yields

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{t}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}+\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{v}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{4}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}},

and the result follows.
∎

Remark C.13
.

As before, these estimates are uniform and thus do not depend on
(x_{0},v_{0})
.

Appendix D
Estimates for shifted ODE under additional assumptions

In this section, we shall derive high order error estimates for the shifted ODE method.
As one would expect, our analysis will require
\nabla f
to have better than Lipschitz regularity.
Before proceeding to the main results of this section, we shall prove the following theorems:

Theorem D.1
.

Suppose that
T:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is
M_{2}
-Lipschitz with respect to the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
operator norm and
X_{1},X_{2},Y
are
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued random variables. Then for
p\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\big(T(X_{1})-T(X_{2})\big)Y\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}\leq M_{2}\big\|X_{1}-X_{2}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\big\|Y\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}.

(D.1)

Proof.

The result follows directly from the Lipschitz regularity of
T
and definition of
\|\cdot\|_{2}\hskip 0.7113pt
.

\displaystyle\big\|\big(T(X_{1})-T(X_{2})\big)Y\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\big(T(X_{1})-T(X_{2})\big)Y\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|T(X_{1})-T(X_{2})\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\big\|Y\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[M_{2}^{p}\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|X_{1}-X_{2}\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\big\|Y\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\big\|X_{1}-X_{2}\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big)^{2}\,\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\big\|Y\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big)^{2}\,\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p}},

where the last line was obtained from Hölder’s inequality.
∎

Theorem D.2
.

Let
T:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}
be
M_{2}
-bounded and
M_{3}
-Lipschitz with respect to the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
operator norm and
X_{1},X_{2},Y_{1},Y_{2}
be
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-valued random variables. Then for
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\big\|T(X_{1})\big(Y_{1},Y_{2}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\big\|Y_{2}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(D.2)

\displaystyle\big\|\big(T(X_{1})-T(X_{2})\big)\big(Y_{1},Y_{2}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{3}\big\|X_{1}-X_{2}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2p}}\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4p}}\big\|Y_{2}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4p}},

(D.3)

Proof.

The first estimate can be shown from Hölder’s inequality and the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{2}
.

\displaystyle\big\|T(X_{1})\big(Y_{1},Y_{2}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|T(X_{1})\big(Y_{1},Y_{2}\big)\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|T(X_{1})\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\big\|Y_{2}\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[M_{2}^{p}\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\big\|Y_{2}\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{2}^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|Y_{2}\big\|_{2}^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p}}.

The second bound follows by essentially the same argument as the proof of Theorem
D.1
.

\displaystyle\big\|\big(T(X_{1})-T(X_{2})\big)\big(Y_{1},Y_{2}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{p}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|\big(T(X_{1})-T(X_{2})\big)\big(Y_{1},Y_{2}\big)\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|T(X_{1})-T(X_{2})\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{2}^{p}\big\|Y_{2}\,\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{3}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|X_{1}-X_{2}\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{2}^{p}\,\big\|Y_{2}\big\|_{2}^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{3}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|X_{1}-X_{2}\big\|_{2}^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{2}^{2p}\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|Y_{2}\big\|_{2}^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq M_{3}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|X_{1}-X_{2}\big\|_{2}^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|Y_{1}\big\|_{2}^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4p}}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\|Y_{2}\big\|_{2}^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4p}},

where we applied Hölder’s inequality in the last two lines.
∎

Remark D.3
.

We shall use Theorem
D.1
with
T=\nabla^{2}f
and Theorem
D.2
with
T=\nabla^{3}f
.
This will be fruitful as we already have
\mathbb{L}_{p}
estimates with
p\in\{2,4,8\}
from Appendix
C
.

D.1. The Hessian of
f
is Lipschitz

In this subsection, we make the assumptions:

\bullet

The function
f
is twice continuously differentiable and
m
-strongly convex where

\circ
the gradient
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz continuous,

\circ
and the Hessian
\nabla^{2}f
is
M_{2}
-Lipschitz continuous.

\bullet

The underdamped Langevin diffusion
(x,v)
has an initial condition
(x_{0},v_{0})\sim\pi
,
where
\pi
denotes the stationary distribution. It follows that
(x_{t},v_{t})\sim\pi
for
t\geq 0
.

Since we are imposing further regularity on
f
, the results from Appendix
C
still hold.
To begin, we will estimate the “
\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f\hskip 0.7113pt
” integral which appears in the expansion of
\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-v_{t}\big)
.

Theorem D.4
.

For each
n\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq C_{7}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}},

(D.4)

where the constant
C_{7}
is defined as

\displaystyle C_{7}

\displaystyle:=M_{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{6}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{3}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|\wideparen{v}|}(4)+\frac{4}{35}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{4}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{x}}(4)+\frac{1}{6}\hskip 0.7113pt\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{x}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(4)\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+Md^{\hskip 0.7113pt-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{8}{105}\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{4}(2)+\frac{1}{6}\gamma\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(2)+\frac{1}{24}uM\hskip 0.7113ptC_{3}(2)h_{n}\bigg).

Proof.

Applying Minkowski’s inequality to the expansion (
A.5
) in Theorem
A.12
gives

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})\big)\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\,ds\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s})\,ds\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\sigma\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},s}\big)\,ds\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\sigma\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\,ds\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}.

We shall estimate each of these terms using results from Appendix
C
and Theorem
D.1
.

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})\big)\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\,ds\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\hskip-4.2679pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})\big)\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq M_{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-x_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{1}{6}M_{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{3}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|\wideparen{v}|}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}},

where the final line follows by the local estimate (
C.2
) and the global bound (
C.12
).
Similarly, by (
C.3
) and the growth estimate (
C.18
), the second term can be estimated as

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s})\,ds\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq M_{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{4}{35}M_{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{4}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{x}}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}.

The third term can be directly estimated without the Lipschitz assumption on
\nabla^{2}f
.

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\sigma\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},s}\big)\,ds\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\sigma\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},s}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\sigma\widetilde{B}_{t_{n},s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\leq M\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\bigg\|\gamma\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\Big(v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\Big)\,dr+u\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{r})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})\big)\,dr\bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}ds\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\gamma M\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptv_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds\,dt+\gamma\sigma M\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|H_{n}+6K_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+uM^{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113ptx_{r}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{105}\gamma M\hskip 0.7113ptC_{4}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}+\frac{1}{6}\gamma\sigma M\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}+\frac{1}{24}uM^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{3}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{9}{2}},

where the upper bound
\big\|\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq M
follows from the Lipschitz regularity of
\nabla f
.
Just as for the second term, we will estimate the last term using Theorems
C.12
and
D.1
.

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\sigma\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\,ds\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sigma\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{s})-\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\sigma M_{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|x_{s}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\big\|H_{n}+6K_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{1}{6}\sigma M_{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{x}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}},

and the result follows.
∎

Using Theorem
D.4
, we can derive higher order estimates for the ODE approximation.

Theorem D.5
.

For
n\geq 0
and
\lambda\in[0,\gamma]
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq C_{8}(\lambda)\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}},

(D.5)

where the constant
C_{8}(\lambda)
is given by

\displaystyle C_{8}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=u\hskip 0.7113ptC_{7}+\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{4}{35}\gamma^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{2}(2)+\frac{1}{6}uMC_{3}(2)+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(2)+\frac{1}{24}\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptuMC_{3}(2)\hskip 0.7113pth_{n}\bigg).

Proof.

Just as for Theorem
C.5
, we shall use the expansion (
C.7
) of
(\lambda x+v)-(\lambda x^{\prime}+v^{\prime})
.

\displaystyle\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\Bigg(\gamma^{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big)\,dr\,ds\,dt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 75.3998pt+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma(h_{n})^{3}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)+\gamma u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{r})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})\big)\,dr\,ds\,dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt.

Using Minkowski’s inequality and the Lipschitz regularity of
\nabla f
, we have the
\mathbb{L}_{2}
estimate:

\displaystyle\Big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\gamma^{2}\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds\,dt+uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|x_{s}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{K}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}+\gamma uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|x_{r}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+u\,\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt\,\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}.

Therefore by Theorem
D.4
along with the local estimates (
C.1
) and (
C.2
), it follows that

\displaystyle\Big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq u\hskip 0.7113ptC_{6}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}+\frac{4}{35}\gamma^{2}\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{2}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}+\frac{1}{6}uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{3}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{K}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}}+\frac{1}{24}\gamma uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{3}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{9}{2}},

which gives the desired result.
∎

Theorem D.6
.

Let
\big\{y_{n}^{\prime}\big\}
be the error process defined in Theorem
B.5
. Then for
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq C_{8}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{7}{2}},

(D.6)

where the constant
C_{8}
is defined as

\displaystyle C_{8}:=2u\hskip 0.7113ptC_{6}+\frac{1}{6}\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\gamma^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{2}(2)+uMC_{3}(2)+\gamma^{2}\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(2)+\frac{1}{4}\gamma\hskip 0.7113ptuMC_{3}(2)\hskip 0.7113pth_{n}\bigg).

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Minkowski’s inequality and Theorem
D.5
.
∎

Using the above, we arrive at our second global error estimate for the shifted ODE.

Theorem D.7

(Global error estimate for the shifted ODE with a Lipschitz Hessian)
.

Suppose the approximation
\big\{(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n})\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
was obtained using a constant step size
h>0
.
Let
\big\{y_{n}\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
be the error process between
(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}\hskip 0.7113pt)
and the true diffusion
(x,v)
defined in Theorem
B.1
.
Then under the assumptions detailed at the start of this subsection, we have

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\frac{1-e^{-n\alpha h}}{1-e^{-\alpha h}}\,C_{7}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{7}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(D.7)

for
n\geq 0
, where the contraction rate
\alpha
is given by

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{\big((\gamma-\lambda)^{2}-uM\big)\vee\big(um-\lambda^{2}\big)}{\gamma-2\lambda}\,.

Proof.

The result follows using exactly the same argument as the proof of Theorem
C.8
.
The only difference is that we use Theorem
D.6
to estimate
y^{\prime}
instead of Theorem
C.6
.
∎

Just as in Appendix
C
, we can deduce the order of convergence for the shifted ODE.

Corollary D.8
.

Suppose that the underdamped Langevin diffusion
\big\{(x_{t},v_{t})\big\}_{t\geq 0}
and the shifted ODE approximation
\big\{(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n})\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
have the same initial velocity. Then for
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq\sqrt{\lambda^{2}+(\gamma-\lambda)^{2}}\,e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{\alpha}+h\Big)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{7}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{5}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt.

(D.8)

Proof.

Applying the inequalities
e^{-n\alpha h}\geq 0
and
\alpha h\leq(1+\alpha h)(1-e^{-\alpha h})
to (
D.7
) yields

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{\alpha}+h\Big)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{7}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{5}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Since we assume that
\widetilde{v}_{0}=v_{0}
(which is achievable in practice as
v_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,u\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d})
), we have

\displaystyle\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{0}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{0}+v_{0}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|\big((\gamma-\lambda)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{0}\big)-\big((\gamma-\lambda)\hskip 0.7113ptx_{0}+v_{0}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt(\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|(\gamma-\lambda)(\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\lambda^{2}+(\gamma-\lambda)^{2}\Big)\big\|\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2},

and the result follows.
∎

D.2. The Hessian and third derivative of
f
are Lipschitz

We now assume that

\bullet

The function
f
is three times continuously differentiable and
m
-strongly convex where

\circ
the gradient
\nabla f
is
M
-Lipschitz continuous,

\circ
the Hessian
\nabla^{2}f
is
M_{2}
-Lipschitz continuous,

\circ
and the third derivative
\nabla^{3}f
is
M_{3}
-Lipschitz continuous.

\bullet

The underdamped Langevin diffusion
(x,v)
has an initial condition
(x_{0},v_{0})\sim\pi
,
where
\pi
denotes the stationary distribution. So just as before,
(x_{t},v_{t})\sim\pi
for
t\geq 0
.

Since this includes previous assumptions, the results from Appendices
C
and
D.1
hold.
Our approach will follow the same strategy as the proof of Theorem 1 in
[
39
]
where estimates are obtained using both local mean-square and mean deviation error estimates.
Since we have already obtained good
\mathbb{L}_{2}
estimates, we shall focus on mean deviation errors.

Theorem D.9

(High order Taylor expansions)
.

For all
n\geq 0
and
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla f(x_{t})

\displaystyle=\nabla f(x_{t_{n}})+\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(x_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big)+\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(x_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big)\,dx_{t}+R_{n}(t),

\displaystyle\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})

\displaystyle=\nabla f(x_{t_{n}})+\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big)+\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big)\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}+\wideparen{R}_{n}(t),

where the remaining terms
R_{n}(t)
and
\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)
satisfy the following estimates:

\displaystyle\big\|R_{n}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{8}^{(1)}d^{\frac{3}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{3},

(D.9)

\displaystyle\big\|\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{8}^{(2)}d^{\frac{3}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{3},

(D.10)

with the constants
C_{8}^{(1)}
and
C_{8}^{(2)}
defined as

\displaystyle C_{8}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{6}M_{3}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{x}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|v|}^{2}(8)\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle C_{8}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{6}M_{3}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{x}}(4)\Big(C_{|\wideparen{v}|}(8)+\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(8)\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)^{2}.

Proof.

We first recall the Taylor expansions (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) given by Theorem
A.13
.

\displaystyle\nabla f(x_{t})

\displaystyle=\nabla f(x_{t_{n}})+\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(x_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big)+\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(x_{s}-x_{t_{n}}\big)\,dx_{s}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{r})-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\,dx_{r}\,dx_{s}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})

\displaystyle=\nabla f(x_{t_{n}})+\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t_{n}}\big)+\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-x_{t_{n}}\big)\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}\hskip 0.7113pt.

We can estimate the remainder term
R_{n}
using the bounds from Theorems
C.11
and
C.12
.

\displaystyle\big\|R_{n}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{r})-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(v_{r},v_{s}\big)\,dr\,ds\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{r})-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(v_{r},v_{s}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\leq M_{3}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|x_{r}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|v_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{8}}\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|v_{s}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{8}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}M_{3}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{x}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|v|}^{2}(8)\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\frac{3}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{3},

where the penultimate line follows from Theorem
D.2
. Similarly, we can estimate
\wideparen{R}_{n}
as

\displaystyle\big\|\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\Bigg\|\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big),\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\,dr\,ds\hskip 0.7113pt\Bigg\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big)\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big),\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq M_{3}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-x_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{8}}\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{8}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{1}{6}M_{3}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{x}}(4)\Big(C_{|\wideparen{v}|}(8)+\sigma\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(8)\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)^{2}d^{\frac{3}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{3},

which gives the desired result.
∎

We now turn our attention to the mean deviation error between
\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})
and
\nabla f(x_{t})
.

Theorem D.10
.

For
n\geq 0
and
t\in[t_{n},t_{n+1}]
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\Big]\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq C_{8}\,d^{\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{3}+C_{9}\,d\hskip 0.7113pth_{n}(t-t_{n})^{2},

(D.11)

where
\displaystyle C_{8}:=C_{8}^{(1)}+C_{8}^{(2)}
and the constant
C_{9}
is given by

\displaystyle C_{9}

\displaystyle:=M_{2}\bigg(\frac{2}{9}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{v}}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{v}}(4)+\frac{14}{25}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{v}}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(4)+\frac{1}{2}\big(C_{K}(4)\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\big(C_{v}(4)\big)^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{3}MC_{5}(p)d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-\frac{1}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{3}M_{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{5}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|v|}(4)(h_{n})^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Proof.

It follows from the Taylor expansions presented in Theorem
D.9
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{n}\Bigg[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big)+\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-x_{t_{n}}\big)\,d\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(x_{s}-x_{t_{n}}\big)\,dx_{s}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)-R_{n}(t)\big]

\displaystyle=\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\,\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big]-\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(v_{r},v_{s}\big)\Big]\,dr\,ds+\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)\big]-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[R_{n}(t)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big),\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\Big]\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{t_{n}}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big),\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{t_{n}}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\Big]\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(v_{t_{n}},\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\Big]\,dr\,ds+\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{r}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big),v_{t_{n}}\big)\Big]\,dr\,ds-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[R_{n}(t)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(v_{r}-v_{t_{n}},v_{s}-v_{t_{n}}\big)\Big]\,dr\,ds+\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\,\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big]

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{t_{n}}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big),\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{t_{n}}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\Big]\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(v_{t_{n}},\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big]\big)\,dr\,ds+\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{r}\big],v_{t_{n}}\big)\,dr\,ds-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[R_{n}(t)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(v_{r}-v_{t_{n}},v_{s}-v_{t_{n}}\big)\Big]\,dr\,ds+\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\,\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big].

Since we can estimate the
\mathbb{L}_{2}
norm of conditional expectations by Theorem
A.6
and bounded linear operators by Theorems
D.1
and
D.2
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\Big]\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{t_{n}}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big),\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{t_{n}}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(v_{t_{n}},\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big]\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds+\big\|R_{n}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{r}\big],v_{t_{n}}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds+\big\|\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla^{3}f(x_{t_{n}})\big(v_{r}-v_{t_{n}},v_{s}-v_{t_{n}}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds+\big\|\nabla^{2}f(x_{t_{n}})\,\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq M_{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{t_{n}}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{t_{n}}+\sigma\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,dr\,ds

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+M_{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s}-v_{s}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,dr\,ds+\big\|R_{n}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+M_{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}-v_{r}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,dr\,ds+\big\|\wideparen{R}_{n}(t)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+M_{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|v_{r}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\big\|v_{s}-v_{t_{n}}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{4}}\,dr\,ds+M\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\hskip 0.7113pt\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t}-x_{t}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{2}{9}M_{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{v}}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{v}}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{3}+\frac{14}{25}M_{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{\wideparen{v}}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{K}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{\frac{5}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{2}M_{2}\big(C_{K}(4)\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pth_{n}(t-t_{n})^{2}+\frac{1}{3}M_{2}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{5}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{|v|}(4)\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{4}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{6}M_{2}\big(C_{v}(4)\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(t-t_{n})^{3}+\frac{1}{3}MC_{5}(p)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{3}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\Big(C_{8}^{(1)}+C_{8}^{(2)}\Big)d^{\frac{3}{2}}(t-t_{n})^{3},

where we used estimates from Theorems
C.1
,
C.4
,
C.11
,
C.12
and
D.9
in the last line.
∎

Theorem D.11
.

For
n\geq 0
and
\lambda\in[0,\gamma]
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\Big]\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq C_{10}(\lambda)\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{4},

(D.12)

where the constant
C_{10}(\lambda)
is given by

\displaystyle C_{10}(\lambda):=\frac{1}{4}\hskip 0.7113ptu\hskip 0.7113ptC_{8}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}u\hskip 0.7113ptC_{9}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{24}\hskip 0.7113pt\gamma uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{3}(2)d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-1}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+u\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\bigg(\frac{1}{20}C_{8}+\frac{1}{12}C_{9}\,d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)h_{n}+\frac{1}{60}\hskip 0.7113pt\gamma^{2}\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{5}(2)\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt-1}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Proof.

Just as for Theorem
D.5
, we shall use the expansion (
C.7
) of
(\lambda x+v)-(\lambda x^{\prime}+v^{\prime})
.

\displaystyle\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt=\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\Bigg(\gamma^{2}\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big)\,dr\,ds\,dt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big)\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 75.3998pt+\frac{1}{6}\gamma^{2}\sigma(h_{n})^{3}\big(H_{n}+6K_{n}\big)+\gamma u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(x_{r})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r})\big)\,dr\,ds\,dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big(\hskip 0.7113pt\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big)\,dt.

Using Minkowski’s inequality and the Lipschitz regularity of
\nabla f
, we have the
\mathbb{L}_{2}
estimate:

\displaystyle\Big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\Big]\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\gamma^{2}\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[v_{r}-\wideparen{v}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+u\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\nabla f(x_{s})-\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{s})\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\gamma uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\hskip-4.2679pt\int_{t_{n}}^{t}\,\int_{t_{n}}^{s}\big\|x_{r}-\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{r}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dr\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+u\int_{t_{n}}^{t_{n+1}}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\nabla f(\wideparen{x}^{\hskip 0.7113ptn}_{t})-\nabla f(x_{t})\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\,dt.

Therefore by Theorem
D.10
along with the local estimates (
C.2
) and (
C.4
), it follows that

\displaystyle\Big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}+v_{t_{n+1}}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}+v_{t_{n+1}}^{\prime}\big)\Big]\Big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq\frac{1}{4}u\hskip 0.7113ptC_{8}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{4}+\frac{1}{3}u\hskip 0.7113ptC_{9}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{4}+\frac{1}{24}\gamma uM\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{3}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{9}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{20}u\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{8}\,d^{\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{5}+\frac{1}{12}u\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{9}\,d\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{5}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{60}\gamma^{2}\big(\gamma-\lambda\big)C_{5}(2)\sqrt{d\hskip 0.7113pt}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{11}{2}},

which gives the desired result.
∎

Theorem D.12
.

Let
\big\{y_{n}^{\prime}\big\}
be the error process given by Theorem
B.5
. Then for
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq C_{10}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{4},

(D.13)

where the constant
C_{10}
is defined as

\displaystyle C_{10}:=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 0.7113ptu\hskip 0.7113ptC_{8}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{3}u\hskip 0.7113ptC_{9}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{24}\hskip 0.7113pt\gamma^{2}uMC_{3}(2)d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-1}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+u\gamma\bigg(\frac{1}{20}C_{8}+\frac{1}{12}C_{9}\,d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)h_{n}+\frac{1}{60}\hskip 0.7113pt\gamma^{3}C_{5}(2)\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt-1}\hskip 0.7113pt(h_{n})^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Minkowski’s inequality and Theorem
D.11
.
∎

Using this theorem, we arrive at our final global error estimate for the shifted ODE.
As stated before, we shall follow the same strategy of the proof as for Theorem 1 in
[
39
]
.

Theorem D.13

(Global error estimate for the shifted ODE with Lipschitz Hessian and third derivative)
.

Suppose
\big\{(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n})\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
was obtained using a constant step size
h>0
.
Let
\big\{y_{n}\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
be the error process between
(\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x},\widetilde{v}\hskip 0.7113pt)
and the true diffusion
(x,v)
defined in Theorem
B.1
.
Then under the assumptions detailed at the start of this subsection, we have

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\frac{1-e^{-n\alpha h}}{1-e^{-\alpha h}}\,C_{11}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\hskip 0.7113pth^{7}\hskip 0.7113pt,

(D.14)

for
n\geq 0
, where the constant
C_{11}
is defined as

\displaystyle C_{11}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{\alpha}\big(C_{10}\big)^{2}+\big(C_{8}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt-1}+\frac{2}{\alpha}e^{2\alpha h}\big(C_{1}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pth\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt-1},

and contraction rate
\alpha
is given by

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{\big((\gamma-\lambda)^{2}-uM\big)\vee\big(um-\lambda^{2}\big)}{\gamma-2\lambda}\,.

Proof.

Applying Theorem
A.7
to
X=y_{n+1}^{\prime},Y=\widetilde{y}_{n+1},Z=y_{n}
and
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{n}
produces

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}+\widetilde{y}_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\big\|\widetilde{y}_{n+1}-y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{c}{h}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\Big(1+\frac{h}{c}\Big)\big\|\widetilde{y}_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

Note that
y_{n+1}^{\prime}+\widetilde{y}_{n+1}=y_{n+1}
and by Theorem
A.6
along with the Tower law, we have

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}-2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle y_{n+1}^{\prime},\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}-2\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big\langle y_{n+1}^{\prime},\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\rangle\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}-\big\|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+2\hskip 0.7113pt\big\|y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\big\|\widetilde{y}_{n+1}-y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\frac{c}{h}\big\|\hskip 0.7113pt\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[y_{n+1}^{\prime}\big]\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\Big(1+\frac{h}{c}\Big)\big\|\widetilde{y}_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

Applying previous results (Theorems
B.1
,
B.4
,
D.6
and
D.12
) yields the local estimate:

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\big(C_{8}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt2}+c\hskip 0.7113pt\big(C_{10}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\bigg)h^{7}+2\hskip 0.7113ptC_{1}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{8}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{9}{2}}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\Big(1+\frac{h}{c}\Big)e^{-2\alpha h}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle 2\hskip 0.7113ptC_{1}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{8}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pth^{\frac{9}{2}}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle=2\hskip 0.7113pt\bigg(\sqrt{\widetilde{c}\,}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{1}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{8}\hskip 0.7113ptd\hskip 0.7113pth^{4}\bigg)\Bigg(\frac{\sqrt{h}}{\sqrt{\widetilde{c}\,}}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{c}\hskip 0.7113pt\big(C_{1}\hskip 0.7113ptC_{8}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt2}\hskip 0.7113pth^{8}+\frac{h}{\widetilde{c}}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2},

where
\widetilde{c}>0
is some constant to be determined. Hence

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\Big(1+\widetilde{c}\hskip 0.7113pt\big(C_{1}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pth\Big)\big(C_{8}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt2}+c\hskip 0.7113pt\big(C_{10}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\bigg)h^{7}+\bigg(\Big(1+\frac{h}{c}\Big)e^{-2\alpha h}+\frac{h}{\widetilde{c}}\bigg)\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

Setting
c=\frac{2}{\alpha}
and
\widetilde{c}=\frac{2}{\alpha}e^{2\alpha h}
gives

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n+1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\Big(1+\frac{2}{\alpha}e^{2\alpha h}\big(C_{1}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pth\Big)\big(C_{8}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt2}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\big(C_{10}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\bigg)h^{7}+\Big(1+\alpha h\Big)e^{-2\alpha h}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\Big(1+\frac{2}{\alpha}e^{2\alpha h}\big(C_{1}\big)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pth\Big)\big(C_{8}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt2}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\big(C_{10}\big)^{2}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\bigg)h^{7}+e^{-\alpha h}\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

Finally, we can apply the above inequality
n
times to derive the required estimate for
y_{n}
.

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha h}\big\|y_{n-1}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+C_{11}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\hskip 0.7113pth^{7}

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69054pt\vdots

\displaystyle\leq e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\Big(e^{-k\alpha h}C_{11}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\hskip 0.7113pth^{7}\Big),

and the result follows.
∎

Just as in the previous subsection, we can deduce the convergence rate for the ODE.

Corollary D.14
.

Suppose that the underdamped Langevin diffusion
\big\{(x_{t},v_{t})\big\}_{t\geq 0}
and the shifted ODE approximation
\big\{(\widetilde{x}_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\widetilde{v}_{n})\big\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
have the same initial velocity. Then for
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}\leq\sqrt{\lambda^{2}+(\gamma-\lambda)^{2}}\,e^{-\frac{1}{2}n\alpha h}\big\|\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\bigg(\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{\alpha}+h\Big)C_{11}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pth^{3}.

(D.15)

Proof.

Applying the inequalities
e^{-n\alpha h}\geq 0
and
\alpha h\leq(1+\alpha h)(1-e^{-\alpha h})
to (
D.14
) yields

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}\leq e^{-n\alpha h}\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{\alpha}+h\Big)\hskip 0.7113ptC_{11}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\hskip 0.7113pth^{6}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Since we assume that
\widetilde{v}_{0}=v_{0}
(which is achievable in practice as
v_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,u\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d})
), we have

\displaystyle\big\|y_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{0}\big)-\big(\lambda\hskip 0.7113ptx_{0}+v_{0}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|\big((\gamma-\lambda)\hskip 0.7113pt\widetilde{x}_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{0}\big)-\big((\gamma-\lambda)\hskip 0.7113ptx_{0}+v_{0}\big)\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|\lambda\hskip 0.7113pt(\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\big\|(\gamma-\lambda)(\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0})\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\lambda^{2}+(\gamma-\lambda)^{2}\Big)\big\|\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}.

Substituting this into the above inequality gives

\displaystyle\big\|y_{n}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\lambda^{2}+(\gamma-\lambda)^{2}\hskip 0.7113pt\big)\hskip 0.7113pte^{-n\alpha h}\big\|\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}^{2}+\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{\alpha}+h\Big)C_{11}\hskip 0.7113ptd^{\hskip 0.7113pt3}\hskip 0.7113pth^{6}\hskip 0.7113pt

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\sqrt{\lambda^{2}+(\gamma-\lambda)^{2}}\,e^{-\frac{1}{2}n\alpha h}\big\|\widetilde{x}_{0}-x_{0}\big\|_{\mathbb{L}_{2}}+\bigg(\Big(\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{1}{\alpha}+h\Big)C_{11}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}d^{\hskip 0.7113pt\frac{3}{2}}\hskip 0.7113pth^{3}\Bigg)^{2},

and the result follows.
∎

Appendix E
Generating integrals of Brownian motion

In this section, we shall detail how the stochastic integrals used by the numerical methods for underdamped Langevin dynamics can be generated. In particular, we discuss how one can “combine” integrals defined over intervals
[s,u]
and
[u,t]
to give integrals over
[s,t]
.
To begin, we will consider the integrals
\{W_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptH_{n}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptK_{n}\}_{n\hskip 0.7113pt\geq\hskip 0.7113pt0}
used by the proposed methods.

Definition E.1
.

Let
W
be a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion. For
0\leq s\leq t
, we define

\displaystyle H_{s,t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{t-s}\int_{s}^{t}\Big(W_{s,r}-\frac{r-s}{t-s}\,W_{s,t}\Big)dr\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle K_{s,t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{(t-s)^{2}}\int_{s}^{t}\Big(W_{s,r}-\frac{r-s}{t-s}\,W_{s,t}\Big)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}(t-s)-(r-s)\bigg)dr\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle M_{s,t}

\displaystyle:=\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,r}\,dr\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle N_{s,t}

\displaystyle:=\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)W_{s,r}\,dr\hskip 0.7113pt.

Recall that by Theorem
3.5
, we have that
W_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,hI_{d}\big)
,
H_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{12}hI_{d}\big)
,
K_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}\big(0,\frac{1}{720}hI_{d}\big)
are independent. In our numerical experiment, we generate and “combine” the triples
\big(W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptK_{s,u}\big)
and
\big(W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptH_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptK_{u,t}\big)
to give
\big(W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptH_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,\hskip 0.7113ptK_{s,t}\big)
.

Theorem E.2

(Combining
(W,H,K)
on neighbouring intervals)
.

Let
0\leq s\leq t
. Then

\displaystyle W_{s,t}

\displaystyle=W_{s,u}+W_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle M_{s,t}

\displaystyle=M_{s,u}+M_{u,t}+(t-u)W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle N_{s,t}

\displaystyle=N_{s,u}+N_{u,t}+(u-s)M_{u,t}+\bigg(\frac{1}{2}(t-u)^{2}+(t-u)(u-s)\bigg)W_{s,u}\hskip 0.7113pt.

for
u\in[s,t]
. In addition,
(W\hskip 0.7113pt,H\hskip 0.7113pt,K)_{s,t}
can be mapped to
(W\hskip 0.7113pt,M\hskip 0.7113pt,N\hskip 0.7113pt)_{s,t}
and vice versa as

\displaystyle M_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(t-s)W_{s,t}+(t-s)H_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle N_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}(t-s)^{2}\hskip 0.7113ptW_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}(t-s)^{2}H_{s,t}-(t-s)^{2}K_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

The first identity is trivial as
W_{s,t}=W_{t}-W_{s}\hskip 0.7113pt
,
W_{s,u}=W_{u}-W_{s}
and
W_{u,t}=W_{t}-W_{u}\hskip 0.7113pt
.
The identities for
M_{s,t}
and
N_{s,t}
can be shown by direct calculation.

\displaystyle M_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,r}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}W_{s,r}\,dr

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}W_{s,r}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}W_{s,u}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}W_{u,r}\,dr

\displaystyle=M_{s,u}+(t-u)W_{s,u}+M_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

\displaystyle N_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}(r-s)W_{s,r}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}(r-s)W_{s,r}\,dr

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}(r-s)W_{s,r}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}(r-s)W_{s,u}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}(u-s)W_{u,r}\,dr+\int_{u}^{t}(r-u)W_{u,r}\,dr

\displaystyle=N_{s,u}+W_{s,u}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}(t-u)^{2}+(t-u)(u-s)\bigg)+(u-s)M_{u,t}+N_{u,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

It is also straightforward to express
M_{s,t}
and
N_{s,t}
in terms of
W_{s,t}
,
H_{s,t}
and
K_{s,t}
since

\displaystyle(t-s)H_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}W_{s,r}\,dr-\int_{s}^{t}\frac{r-s}{t-s}\,W_{s,t}\,dr=M_{s,t}-\frac{1}{2}(t-s)W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle(t-s)^{2}K_{s,t}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\Big(W_{s,r}-\frac{r-s}{t-s}\,W_{s,t}\Big)\bigg(\frac{1}{2}(t-s)-(r-s)\bigg)dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{t}(t-s)W_{s,r}\,dr-\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)W_{s,r}\,dr

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{t}(r-s)W_{s,t}\,dr+\int_{s}^{t}\frac{(r-s)^{2}}{t-s}\,W_{s,t}\,dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(t-s)M_{s,t}-N_{s,t}+\frac{1}{12}(t-s)^{2}W_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Substituting
M_{s,t}=(t-s)H_{s,t}+\frac{1}{2}(t-s)W_{s,t}
into the above gives the final identity.
∎

We now consider the stochastic integrals used by the numerical methods discussed in Section
4
. These integrals are either of the form
\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-\tau)}dW_{\tau}
or
\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau
.

Theorem E.3

(Distribution of integrals required to simulate physical Brownian motion)
.

Let
0\leq s\leq t
. Then

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-\tau)}dW_{\tau}\\
\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\Bigg(\begin{matrix}0\\[-3.0pt]
0\end{matrix}\Bigg)\,,\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1-e^{-2\gamma h}}{2\gamma}\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d}&\frac{(1-e^{-\gamma h})^{2}}{2\gamma^{2}}\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d}\\[3.0pt]
\frac{(1-e^{-\gamma h})^{2}}{2\gamma^{2}}\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d}&\frac{4e^{-\gamma h}-e^{-2\gamma h}+2\gamma h-3}{2\gamma^{3}}\hskip 0.7113ptI_{d}\end{pmatrix}\right),

where
h:=t-s
.

Proof.

Since each coordinate of
W
is an independent Brownian motion, it suffices to show the result when
d=1
. Since
\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-\tau)}dW_{\tau}
and
\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau
can be obtained as linear functionals of the same Brownian motion, they follow a (joint) normal distribution.
Moreover, by the symmetry of Brownian motion, we see that they both have mean zero. Using Itô’s isometry, the covariance matrix for the integrals is straightforward to compute.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-\tau)}dW_{\tau}\Bigg)^{2}\,\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}e^{-2\gamma(t-\tau)}\hskip 0.7113ptd\tau=\frac{1-e^{-2\gamma h}}{2\gamma}\,.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau\Bigg)^{2}\,\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{s}^{\tau_{1}}e^{-\gamma(\tau_{1}-r)}dW_{r}\int_{s}^{\tau_{2}}e^{-\gamma(\tau_{2}-r)}dW_{r}\Bigg]\hskip 0.7113ptd\tau_{2}\,d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle=2\hskip 0.7113pt\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau_{1}}\hskip-2.84526pt\int_{s}^{\tau_{2}}e^{-\gamma(\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}-2r)}\hskip 0.7113ptdr\,d\tau_{2}\,d\tau_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4e^{-\gamma h}-e^{-2\gamma h}+2\gamma h-3}{2\gamma^{3}}\,.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-\tau)}dW_{\tau}\hskip-0.7113pt\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau\,\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-r)}dW_{r}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\Bigg]\hskip 0.7113ptd\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(t+\tau-2r)}\hskip 0.7113ptdr\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-e^{-\gamma h})^{2}}{2\gamma^{2}}\,.

Using Fubini’s theorem, we could have also written
\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau=\int_{s}^{t}\frac{1-e^{-\gamma(t-r)}}{\gamma}\,dW_{r}
before applying Itô’s isometry (which may have slightly simplified the calculations).
∎

Corollary E.4
.

The stochastic integral
\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{1-e^{-\gamma h}}{\gamma\hskip 0.7113pt(1+e^{-\gamma h})}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-\tau)}dW_{\tau}+X_{s,t}\hskip 0.7113pt,

where

\displaystyle X_{s,t}\sim\mathcal{N}\Bigg(0,\Bigg(\frac{4e^{-\gamma h}-e^{-2\gamma h}+2\gamma h-3}{2\gamma^{3}}-\frac{(1-e^{-\gamma h})^{3}}{2\gamma^{3}\hskip 0.7113pt(1+e^{-\gamma h})}\Bigg)I_{d}\Bigg),

is independent of
\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-\tau)}dW_{\tau}\sim\mathcal{N}\Big(0,\frac{1-e^{-2\gamma h}}{2\gamma}I_{d}\Big)
.

In our numerical experiment, both of these integrals will be generated over the intervals
[s,u]
,
[u,t]
and
[s,t]
. Therefore, just as before, we require a method for “combining” them.

Theorem E.5

(Combining integrals on neighbouring intervals)
.

Let
0\leq s\leq u\leq t
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-r)}\,dW_{r}

\displaystyle=e^{-\gamma(t-u)}\int_{s}^{u}e^{-\gamma(u-r)}\,dW_{r}+\int_{u}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-r)}\,dW_{r}\hskip 0.7113pt,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau+\int_{u}^{t}\int_{u}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\bigg(\frac{1-e^{-\gamma(t-u)}}{\gamma}\bigg)\int_{s}^{u}e^{-\gamma(u-r)}\,dW_{r}\hskip 0.7113pt.

Proof.

The first identity is trivial as
e^{-\gamma(t-r)}=e^{-\gamma(t-u)}\hskip 0.7113pte^{-\gamma(u-r)}
. For the second, we have

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau+\int_{u}^{t}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{u}\int_{s}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau+\int_{u}^{t}\int_{u}^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int_{u}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau.

The result now follows as
\displaystyle\int_{u}^{t}\int_{s}^{u}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau=\int_{u}^{t}e^{-\gamma(\tau-u)}\,d\tau\int_{s}^{u}e^{-\gamma(u-r)}\,dW_{r}\hskip 0.7113pt.

∎

In the numerical experiment for the randomized midpoint method, it will also be necessary to generate uniform random variables within the intervals
[s,u]
,
[u,t]
and
[s,t]
.
To simply the generation of subsequent stochastic integrals, we use the following procedure:

Step 1.
Generate independent randomized midpoints
x\sim U[s,u]
and
y\sim U[u,t]
.

Step 2.
Generate an independent Rademacher random variable
r
.

Step 3.
Let
z:=\Bigg\{\begin{matrix}x\,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\,r=+1\\[-3.0pt]
y\,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\,r=-1\end{matrix}\,
. (i.e.
z\sim U[s,t]
is the randomized midpoint for
[s,t]
).

Step 4.
Generate the pair
\big(\int_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-3.0)\circle*{2.0}\end{picture}\ }^{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-3.0)\circle*{2.0}\end{picture}\ }e^{-\gamma(t-\tau)}dW_{\tau},\int_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-3.0)\circle*{2.0}\end{picture}\ }^{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-3.0)\circle*{2.0}\end{picture}\ }\int_{\,\begin{picture}(-1.0,-3.0)\circle*{2.0}\end{picture}\ }^{\tau}e^{-\gamma(\tau-r)}dW_{r}\,d\tau\big)
over the intervals

\big([s,x],[x,u],[s,u]\big)
,
\big([u,y],[y,t],[u,t]\big)
and
\big([s,z],[z,t],[s,t]\big)
using Theorems
E.3
and
E.5
.
